# Drashti

> Drashti is built independently for use at a BAPS mandir. It is not an official BAPS product.

Drashti (v1.0, package `drashti`) is a presentation app for BAPS mandirs. It replaces ProPresenter 6 on the mandir's Mac and ProPresenter 7 on its Windows PC, on the same machines and the same screens. The plan is in `PLAN.md` at the workspace root.

Stack: Electron 44, React 19, TypeScript 6 (strict), Zustand, Tailwind 4, SQLite (better-sqlite3), Vitest, Playwright, electron-builder and pnpm 12.

## Guides

- [The operator's guide](docs/operator-guide.md): a sabha in Pro Mode, from switching on to switching off.
- [The presenter's guide](docs/presenter-guide.md): a phone, tablet or laptop as a presenter's remote or the stage's display.
- [The admin's guide](docs/admin-guide.md): setting Drashti up and looking after it.
- [The setup day](docs/setup-day.md): the setup day's checklist, from the audit to the fallback drill, and what to write down.
- [The parallel run](docs/parallel-run.md): the volunteers' guide to trying Drashti at the mandir, with the key card.
- [The Windows checks](docs/windows-checks.md): what to check on the mandir's Windows PC.

The first four are inside Drashti too, without the internet: **Help › Guides…** (Simple Mode shows the operator's and the presenter's). `src/main/guide.test.ts` keeps this list whole, and keeps the key card and the operator's guide true to the keys both ways.

## Status

Phase 0 (foundations) is in place:

- the read-only **audit kit** for the two ProPresenter machines (`tools/audit/`);
- the **show engine** in the main process: versioned, serializable state sent as snapshots and patches through a transport interface;
- the **SQLite library** with the full core model, migrations and two placeholder presentations (only for the tests since Session 50: a new library starts without them);
- **screens**: displays as the OS reports them, screen groups, a canvas size and scaling per screen, and one output window per assigned display, restored on restart;
- one **shared renderer** with bundled Noto fonts, used by the operator preview, the thumbnails and every output;
- a minimal **operator UI** with a single keymap, and an **output watchdog**.

Phase 1 has started:

- text boxes hold **styled runs** (font, size, colour, weight and language per run);
- the **import pipeline** runs in a background worker process: plain-text and Word (`.docx`) lyrics, ProPresenter 6 and 7 presentations, templates and themes, playlists and bundles, and media files, with re-import rules, a media folder that stores each file once, and a report kept for every import;
- **importing from the operator window**: drag files or folders onto the library's presentation list (its sheet, from **Library**) or anywhere on the window, or use **Import…**; progress, then a migration report with a fix for each item; removing presentations with Delete, and Undo;
- **arrangements** set the order a presentation plays in (a repeated chorus shows and plays each time), chosen per presentation or per playlist item;
- **playlists** in the operator window: imported ones with their folders, headers, media and placeholders, and the operator's own, built by dragging from the library or with Add to playlist, and put in order with Up and Down;
- **search** as you type, by title and slide text in English, Gujarati, Hindi and transliteration, ignoring Latin accents, with v and w alike and a doubled vowel as its single one ("dvitiyah" finds "dwitiyah", "Shri" finds "Shree"; Session 13), x as ksh, f as ph, and ru as ri after a consonant ("Axar" finds "Akshar", "Fal" finds "Phal", "Krushna" finds "Krishna", but "guru" never finds "giri"; Session 14) (about 4 to 5 ms a query at 5,000 presentations); text in legacy fonts is left out, and the results say so;
- **editing words as plain text**: any presentation's words in the lyrics format, saved back with each slide's look, backgrounds and cues kept, the live slide updated and Undo; "New…" makes a presentation from pasted words;
- **Set Up Screens…**, a setup wizard: on the first start (and from View > Set Up Screens…), straight on the displays (Session 44): each output's number on it, what it shows (audience, stage or nothing, one click each) and its languages, set by **Done** with a test slide on every screen; **More setup…** goes on to the sound output with a test tone and the default theme, applied at Finish; skipping changes nothing, and Simple Mode never shows it. With a display free and no hall screen yet, the window offers **Show on** it (Session 44): one click;
- **sabha templates**: running orders with headers, fixed items and named slots, kept apart from the week's playlists so they are never run; Save as template on any playlist, New playlist from a template, slots filled through search starting at their category (or asking for a Shastra passage), renamed and given another category; items that start, reset or show timers as they go up (Session 12), kept by templates; two example templates to edit;
- **kirtan details and the library**: category (Drashti's list, which can be added to), kavi, raag, occasions and a recording from the media library; the library filters by them, and search finds kirtans by kavi and raag;
- **auto-transliteration**: Gujarati and Devanagari to Roman letters by rule, as words are said ("ghar", not "ghara"), plain or with accent marks (ISO 15919); "Make transliteration" fills a kirtan's track, keeps lines changed by hand unless told to replace them, and is undone with Undo;
- **each screen's own languages**: every screen group chooses which of a kirtan's languages it shows and in what order (Audience Gujarati and transliteration, the stage Gujarati only, a stream transliteration and English), and the words close up with no gap;
- **the Shastra module** (Session 12): texts an admin loads from authorised sources in a documented file format (`docs/shastra-format.md`), found by reference ("SD 14", "SD 14-16", "Vach G.Pr. 1", with each text's own abbreviations), by their words in any language, or by browsing; a passage shows as slides with its reference line, in its text's theme, cut to fit, in each group's languages (Sanskrit in Devanagari or Gujarati script among them) or as a lower third; passages in playlists and template slots, and put up from a phone or the API;
- **the idle rotation** (Session 12): darshan pictures from the media library and the admin's quotes (in Gujarati, Hindi and English, with an attribution), each up for a set time and dissolving into the next, in step on every screen; each group's Look says whether its screens show it once the operator starts it (it stops by itself when the first slide or picture goes up, so Clear all does not bring it back) or always when nothing is up (a lobby); a quote of the day, one per date, after the pictures and in a stage box; started and stopped from its panel;
- **Samvat and tithi** (Session 12): calendars an admin loads from authorised sources in a documented file format (`docs/calendar-format.md`; Drashti computes no tithi), giving each date's Vikram Samvat year, month, paksha, tithi and festivals in Gujarati and English; today's shows along the bottom of the operator window (Simple Mode too), on stage screens (a Samvat box, or a clock box with the date) and in messages with a Samvat field on the audience screens, changing at midnight; a date no calendar gives shows nothing;
- **Lower Thirds** (Session 44, in Macros' place): a name, a title and a place across the bottom of the hall's screens, the stream or both, in a look of its own, coming in and going out with one of 279 animations found by their plain names; one button shows and hides it, and Clear all, Put it back and Black-out treat it as they treat the slide (`docs/design.md` §44);
- **a MIDI controller** (Session 11): Learn maps a pad to Next, Back, Clear all, Black-out or Logo (Macros, with their panel, times, cues and remote buttons, left in Session 44; a library's macro data is kept);
- **key and fill outputs** (Session 11): a group's fill and key on two displays, for a video switcher to key the words over a camera;
- **masks** (Session 11): a library of shapes that hide part of a screen or show only what is inside them, as a group's own shape in a Look (never cleared) or on the Masks layer (F8);
- **stage layouts** (Session 11): boxes placed on the stage screen (the current and next slide, notes, the clock, timers, the stage message, the playlist's next items, the time left on a video or sound, the screens' state, fixed text), each stage group's chosen in its Look; Standard is the stage screen as it was;
- **Looks** (Session 11): for each screen group, which layers its screens show, a kirtan's languages, and whether slides are drawn as designed or as a lower third; one Look is live, and switching it (from the main window, a phone or the API) changes every group at once; Standard, made from the groups as they were, keeps every screen as it was;
- **kirtans with language tracks**: a kirtan's Gujarati, Hindi, English and transliteration lines are its slides' own words by language (one copy, so nothing drifts); Edit words has a **By language** view (one language, or all of them, slide by slide, with missing lines marked); a presentation becomes a kirtan, or stops being one, without its words moving; imports with lines in two scripts, or an author or artist field, come in as kirtans;
- **themes**: a look per language (font, size, weight, colour, shadow), the text box's place and alignment, and a background, applied to any number of presentations, or a playlist's, with one Undo; presentations made in Drashti start with the default theme; ten built-in themes (Session 26), every word at least 7:1 against its background;
- **props**: a logo or a line of words that stays up whatever slide is live, made from a picture or words, and imported from both older formats' props files;
- **messages**: templates with fields ("Car {plate} please move", or a live timer), filled in and shown on the audience screens, several at once;
- **timers**: countdowns, count-ups, countdowns to a time of day and clocks, started, paused, reset and edited by the operator, shown on the audience screens and the stage; only start, pause and reset are sent, and each window works out the time;
- a **stage screen** for the performers: current and next text, notes, the clock and a stage-only message, in large text;
- **running a sabha from a playlist**: Next and Previous carry on across items (stepping over headers and placeholders), pictures and videos go up as the background and sounds on the audio layer, the next slide shows beside the live one, outputs load the next slide's images and videos ahead, and restart recovery keeps the place in the playlist;
- **media playback**: library media reaches the windows by id only (`drashti-media://`); slide backgrounds play on the background layer and audio cues on the audio layer; images and videos on slides draw everywhere, with still frames for video thumbnails; every screen shows the same frame of a video, and one audio player makes all the sound, on the output chosen in settings; since Session 52 a slide whose background's file is missing (not found at import, or deleted from the library) leaves the background as it was, never black because of it, and LIVE names the file;
- **media Drashti cannot play** (ProRes, AVI, HEIC and the like) is found at import, marked, and listed in the report; **Convert** (in the report or the media list, a file or all of them) makes a copy that plays with the bundled FFmpeg, one file at a time in the background, and everything that used the original uses the copy, with Undo (the original is never changed); since Session 50 a video or sound the player cannot play is converted by itself after its import (below);
- a rotating **log file** in the data folder, and **Help > Save Diagnostics…** for one file to send after a problem; **Help > Keyboard Shortcuts…** (or **?**) lists every key (Session 25), and holds an admin's switch that turns the single-letter keys B, ., L and ? off (Session 37, WCAG 2.1.4: then they do nothing in either mode, and Black-out works from its button, the Logo from its button and, since Session 50, F12; if the setting cannot be read they stay on, as by default, and the log says why, so the operator window never waits blank for it, Session 38), with no library content;
- **backing up and restoring the library** (File menu): a consistent copy of the library, with the media if wanted, in a folder the operator picks; a restore checks the backup, asks once, keeps the current library under `Backups/` and restarts with nothing live; **scheduled backups** (Session 14) on chosen days at a time into a folder an admin picks, the media copied once into a folder they share, the newest kept, waiting while on air, a missing drive skipped with a warning;
- the **slide editor**: text boxes, shapes (rectangles, rounded rectangles, ellipses, lines), pictures and videos on slides, moved, resized, turned and snapped with guides; several elements (Shift-click, a box dragged round them, or Select all) moved, resized and turned together from one box round them, lined up and spaced out; elements copied, cut and pasted between slides and presentations with their styles, languages and media (Paste puts them where they were, a step down when that place is taken; Paste in place, Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+V, exactly there; Cmd/Ctrl+D duplicates); text styles for a box or its selected words, with full shadows, outlines and shrink-to-fit; typing in place in any language; its own Undo and Redo, each gesture one step; one change for the operator's Undo when saved;
- **Simple Mode**: one screen with big buttons for a volunteer (the playlist, Next and Back, Black-out, Logo, Clear all and Put it back), which cannot change anything, with its own way back to Pro Mode (the word **pro**, or a PIN); Drashti comes back in it after an unexpected stop within 3 hours, and starts in Pro Mode after a quit on purpose (or a stop longer ago) unless PINs are set (Sessions 20 and 21);
- **audio playlists** (Session 14): music on the audio layer, one track after another, independent of the slides (before the sabha), with play, pause, next and previous, going round again and shuffle, short fades, in step with the engine's clock; Simple Mode, the remote and the API play and pause it;
- **playback markers** (Session 14): a video's or sound's start and end points and named markers, kept with the media item; a background loops between its points, and since Session 52 a video on a slide, a prop's and the logo's play between them too; a jump to a marker from under the live picture, the remote or the API lands in step on every screen and the audio player; read from the older formats' imports, to be looked over;
- **PDF, PowerPoint and Keynote as pictures** (Session 15): a `.pdf`, `.pptx`, `.ppt` or `.key` dropped on the library (or chosen with Import…) becomes a presentation whose slides each hold one page as a picture over the whole slide, at the first audience group's canvas size (a page of another shape is fitted with black either side), with the speaker notes as the slide's notes; PowerPoint and Keynote files are first saved as PDF by PowerPoint (where it is installed) or Keynote (on a Mac), out of sight and closed again afterwards, and animations show as each slide's finished picture; otherwise the report says to save it as PDF. Session 16 tried them for real on the dev Mac (Keynote 14.4, PowerPoint 16.97) and made them safe: see "Keynote and PowerPoint, for real" below;
- **accessibility** (Session 15, WCAG 2.2 AA): a whole sabha runs by keyboard alone in Pro Mode and in Simple Mode; Tab goes through the operator window region by region as it is laid out (the header, playlists (and the library while its sheet is open), slides, live, show controls, status), every stop with its focus ring; keys a control uses itself (the arrows between tabs, through a menu, on a splitter) never also move the slide on the screens; a row's menu opens from the keyboard (Shift+F10) on the Mac too; every control can be reached with the text at 200%; reducing motion stops the interface's own movement; the phone pages' controls are at least 24 × 24; see `docs/design.md`, section 8;
- **guides** (Session 15): `docs/operator-guide.md` (a sabha in Pro Mode, start to end), `docs/admin-guide.md` (setting Drashti up and looking after it) and `docs/setup-day.md` (the setup day's checklist, and what to write down), in the volunteers' guide's plain words;
- **a three-hour soak test** and **speed on modest hardware** (Session 15): a never-ending sabha on CI's Mac and Windows machines, its memory and processor curves reported (see "Soak test"); and the performance check's heavy cases (video backgrounds up to 4K, masks, dissolves, three outputs with the stream), with and without a handicap (see "Speed on modest hardware");
- **updates** (Session 14): Help > Check for Updates… looks at the releases on GitHub; an admin downloads one (at a gentle speed, waiting while the stream is on air or recording) and says to install it when Drashti quits; Drashti never installs or restarts by itself, and Simple Mode never sees it; a node refused for its version offers to update to Main's; signed and notarised installers when certificates are given to the Release workflow;
- **roles and PINs** (Session 14): off until an admin sets an admin PIN and an operator PIN; then Drashti still starts in Pro Mode, as the operator with no PIN asked (Session 46; in Simple Mode until then), leaving Simple Mode takes a PIN, and setting up (the library, screens, schedules, the stream's settings, phones and nodes, backups and updates) takes the admin PIN, checked in the main process; wrong PINs are limited;
- a **design system** and a redesigned operator window (see "The design system");
- **Drashti's own logo** (Session 19), the lamp's flame, in place of Electron's icon: on the Mac app and its disk image, the Windows installer, Start menu entry, uninstaller and windows, and as the Home Screen and browser icon of the pages phones open (see "The design system");
- **restart recovery** of the whole show: after an unexpected stop (within 3 hours; since Session 20 never after a clean quit) the slide, background, black-out and place in the playlist come back by themselves, with the sound, props, messages, the stage message and running or paused timers (the sound and timers carry on from where they would be), and a slide moving on by itself carries on with the time it had left;
- **transitions** (a cut, or a dissolve on every screen at once, with the background a slide brings) and **auto-advance** (slides moving on by themselves, looping or not, with the time left shown);
- **built-in streaming and recording** (Session 9): the stream's **Program** drawn off screen with the same renderer as the outputs, in two layouts switchable on air (**Camera and words**: the camera or a capture card with the live slide's words as a lower third in the stream group's languages; **Slides**: the hall's picture); one sound input (the mixer's line in) with a level meter and a delay, and Drashti's own sound mixed in on request; **Go live** to YouTube over RTMPS through the bundled FFmpeg (hardware H.264 where the computer has it, x264 otherwise; AAC), with both Go Live and End confirmed; health on screen (time on air, bitrate, frames, dropped frames, connection), reconnecting by itself, and a **recording** to a folder at the same time (or on its own) that a dropped connection never stops; after a crash it goes live again by itself within 5 minutes; keys kept in the system's secure storage and never shown again; ON AIR and REC in the header and in Simple Mode, which cannot start, end or change the stream;
- **the local network** (Session 10, off until the operator turns it on in Pro Mode): phones and tablets on the mandir's Wi-Fi, paired with a QR code or a code that works once, as a **remote** (Next and Back, slides, clears, black-out, the logo, timers and messages, drawn with the outputs' renderer), a **stage display** in a browser (exactly what a stage screen shows), or for **announcements** sent from phones, which reach the screens only once the operator approves them (as a message, or in a ticker scrolling in step on every audience screen); a versioned **API** (`docs/api.md`) for scripts and Companion; every request checked as the windows' are, Simple Mode included;
- **output nodes** (Session 13): the same Drashti, started as a **Node** on another computer (the mandir's second machine, or a small PC by a lobby screen), follows one Main over the local network and shows its screen groups on that computer's displays, drawn by the same renderer on Main's clock (in step to within a frame on the tests' computer), holding its picture when the network drops and catching up by itself; paired with a code Main shows, over TLS with Main's own certificate, which the node pins; copies of the pictures and videos it needs, made ahead and checked by their hash (anything put up before its copy arrived is fetched first, a copy for later giving way, and shows the moment it lands, Main gone and back included); never any sound, and nothing it can change on Main. The **screens dashboard** (the screens line in the status bar, both modes) shows every display on Main and its nodes with a live picture of what it shows, and how each node stands (online, latency, clock, media, late frames, version); the status bar warns when a node goes offline, falls behind on media or drifts out of step; since Session 17 a node saves its own diagnostics (**Help**, **Save Diagnostics…**) and has the watchdog self-test with diagnostics on;
- a **library list** that stays cheap at any size (about 4 ms at 5,000 presentations, kirtan details included), and a **performance check** to run by hand on the real machines.
- **screens and the stream made safe against failures** (Session 23): going live from a Mac (FFmpeg now checks YouTube's certificate against the system's authorities), quitting while on air (one ordered stop list; the stream never resumes after a clean quit), render errors (a screen draws black, the error is logged and the screen reloads once), files that cannot be read (kept aside, never written over), hung and given-up outputs (noticed, restarted, retried, shown as Stopped), and dissolves cut short (never dim, never from black).
- **import from a link** (Sessions 25b and 25c): **File › Import from a Link…** (or the library's **Import…**, **From a Link…**) saves what a link holds in a folder an admin chooses, then imports it through the normal import. **YouTube** (25c): one video as MP4 or MP3, at the quality chosen up to the admin's **Highest video quality** (1080p to begin with), after the permission tick each time; Drashti runs the bundled yt-dlp, which runs YouTube's player JavaScript in the bundled, sandboxed Deno. **Dropbox** (25b): a file or folder link's PowerPoint files, MP4 videos and sounds (sounds since Session 44: a folder's also show in Audio), a video above the setting as a copy that tall; everything else stays in the folder, listed as not taken (see "Import from a Link");
- **Zoom, both ways** (Session 48), with no Zoom SDK, account or paid plan: **Window for Zoom…** (the Stream panel, View menu) opens "Drashti for Zoom", the stream's picture exactly, for Zoom's Share Screen, live or not, from the Program's own frames (the camera opens once); **Show a Window…** (the library's Media) shows another app's window, a Zoom meeting's, live as the hall's background on this computer's screens and the stream, chosen by name with a small picture (never a display or Drashti's own; Screen Recording on a Mac), kept as its last picture with a notice when it closes, never put back after a restart;
- **a video input on the screens** (Session 50): **Add a Video Input…** (the library's Media, Pro Mode) names one of this computer's cameras or capture inputs ("Input 1": a capture card, or the video mixer's program through its USB output), and one click on it in Media's **Video inputs** puts it up as the live background on LIVE, the hall's screens and the stream's Slides layout, dissolving in over a second, the slide over it and no sound; kept on this computer, never in the library or a backup; its last picture held with a notice when it stops; put back after a restart while it is there; phones name it and may clear it, nodes never show it; and since Session 51 a playlist item or a slide's background cue, named by the input's own name, so each computer puts up its own input of that name (one without it says so plainly, and Next goes on past it), and since Session 52 renaming the input in Media takes the library's items and slides that named it to the new name;
- **Audience and Stage** (Session 50): two switches in Pro Mode's top bar (and View › Audience, View › Stage, Cmd+1 and Cmd+2, Ctrl on Windows) take this computer's audience screens (key and fill too), or its stage screens, off so their displays show the computer's desktop, and put them back where they were, while the show, the stream, recording and nodes carry on; off asks first while those screens show something and is never remembered over a restart; Simple Mode, and the status bar's **Turn on**, only turn them on; since Session 51 View shows their keys, an output over the controls asks the question too, and the top bar fits a 1024-wide window with Phones and the PINs on;
- **Keep these screens?** (Session 50): after any change that puts an output on a display (Set Up Screens…'s Done or Finish, Show on, Screens' Use this display or On, a node's display, and since Session 51 Screens' Shows and a key and fill pair's Sends), 15 seconds to **Keep** (Enter) or **Go back** (Esc), counted down in words; with nothing pressed the screens go back as they were (the screens only: the sound, the theme, the stream and the PINs stay as chosen; since Session 51 only what the change changed, and after a crash or a power cut within the 15 seconds too, at the next start), and an output put over the controls asks the same, large in its middle;
- **the audience picture on a stage screen** (Session 50): a stage layout's box, **The audience picture, live**, draws exactly what this computer's first audience group shows (its background, a video input or a window too, the slide, lower third, messages, ticker and picture in picture, black-out and the logo), 16:9 with black around it, under the stage message, the clock and the timers; since Session 51 also on a node with no audience screen of its own (Main's hall, as the live Look sets it) and on a tablet's stage page (from small previews), saying "Audience off" in its corner while Audience is off (since Session 52 its bottom left corner, clear of a stage message along the top); and since Session 52, with no audience group at all, black with "No audience screens", where it drew every layer;
- **the operators' own keys** (Session 50): F5 clears the sound, F6 messages, F7 the ticker and F8 the Masks layer (F5 and F6, and F7 and F8, changed places), F12 is the logo beside L, and Cmd+1 and Cmd+2 (Ctrl on Windows) turn Audience and Stage off and on, as the mandir's operators already press them; F1 to F4, the arrows, B and Esc are as they were; since Session 51 the layer bar's clears go in the order of their keys, F2 to F8, then the Logo and Black-out, every button showing its key;
- **Back 15 s, Forward 15 s and Last 10 s** (Session 50, Pro Mode): for the background video and a single sound under the live picture, and for an audio playlist's track in Music (in the row with its Play and Pause since Session 51), in step everywhere as a jump to a marker is, never past the file's start or end points; since Session 52 every screen's picture lands in step however quickly they are pressed, as the sound has since Session 51;
- **a timer alone on the screens** (Session 50): **Show on the screens** in Timers puts a timer up alone in the slide's place, over the background, fading in and out (Next goes on from where the show was); a **Timer** on a slide in the slide editor (which timer, its look, a last-minute colour, and at zero: hold at 0:00 or go on to the next slide), and **New Timer Slide…** (Timer only, or Timer with words) in the presentation's theme; only the digits that change move;
- **picture in picture** (Session 50): **Picture in Picture…** (the library's Media, Pro Mode) puts the slides, a video input, a picture or video from Media, or a window in a 16:9 box on the audience screens and the stream's Slides layout (in a corner, Small, Medium or Large, with a border, rounded corners, and a fade, scale or slide in); **Swap** and **Take off** under LIVE, where the box can be dragged; a Look can leave it out; its place is remembered in the library; phones may take it off, never set one up; since Session 51 Swap never cuts the main picture (since Session 52 it dissolves in the box's time, never black, where Session 51 moved it with the box), the stream's Camera layout shows the box at its full-frame moments too, and key and fill groups leave it out unless their Look turns it on;
- **every video and sound file** (Session 50): 3GP, FLV, F4V, OGV, TS, VOB, MXF, DV, ASF, DivX and RealMedia videos, and MKA, AC-3, E-AC-3, DTS, CAF, AMR, APE, WavPack, MP2, AU, AIFC, M4B and other sounds, wherever Drashti takes media; each is read with the bundled FFmpeg at import, and what Drashti's player cannot play (an MKV with AC-3 or DTS sound, an AVI, a WMA, an AIFF) is converted by itself, one at a time and never on air, saying **Converting…** and kept off the screens until then; what plays (FLAC, an H.264 MP4) never is; since Session 51 HEVC too where this computer cannot play it, a conversion waiting at a quit starts again by itself at the next start, the drop folder takes sounds, and an original added again after its conversion lands on its copy;
- **no test presentations in a new library** (Session 50): "Language test slides" and "Sample kirtan (placeholder)" are now for the tests only (the example sabha templates and the built-in themes stay); a library that has them loses each at its first start on this Drashti only when it is exactly as Drashti made it and nothing names it (kept 30 days, as anything deleted is); and **Delete…** in the **⋯** menu of the presentation open in the middle (**Delete from the library…** while a playlist item is open there, since Session 51, its question saying any playlist it is in keeps its card, marked **Removed from the library**, and Next goes past it);
- **opening Drashti the first time, shown** (Session 50): the Mac disk image's window is a picture of its two steps (drag Drashti to Applications; the first time, System Settings › Privacy & Security › Open Anyway), as signing is paid (see "Download Drashti").

**Session 50** (22 and 23 Oct 2026; the version stays 1.0.0-alpha.11, and the engine state 12, as no published release read 12) built those eleven parts and mended Session 49's two test faults: `tests/e2e/zoom-window.spec.ts` expects the window for Zoom's first size as Drashti picks it (1280 wide, or the controls' display's work area when narrower, at 16:9) and moves it only to a place that fits a 1024 × 768 display, and `tests/e2e/flash-pixels.spec.ts` reloads a screen during Drashti's longest transition (10 s), so a slow machine has room. Decided: the keys are the ones the operators already press (their program's menus, photographed on 22 Oct 2026); a video input comes on with a fixed one-second dissolve, as the mandir's setup does, makes no sound in the hall, and is kept on its own computer, never in a backup; a playlist item and a slide's background cue for a video input wait (neither is small); Audience and Stage are an operator's switches, never remembered, and Simple Mode only turns them on; Keep these screens? waits 15 s and puts back the screens only; Back 15 s, Forward 15 s and Last 10 s are Pro Mode's (Simple Mode, the phone remote and the keys are as they were); HEVC and pictures are not converted at import; a placeholder presentation goes only when untouched; and nothing is signed, as signing is paid. New end-to-end specs: `video-input`, `audience-stage`, `keep-screens`, `skips`, `timer-slide`, `pip`, `convert-at-import` and `placeholders`, and the audience picture box in `stage-layouts`; how the checks came out: see the final report.

**Session 51** (23 Oct 2026; the version stays 1.0.0-alpha.11 and the engine state 12, as no published release has read 12 yet; the library is at migration 47) mended the three tests that stopped Session 50's release gate, each failing from a fault in Drashti, not in the test (see "The named ones" below): the middle's empty places in CI's 1024 × 645 window, a key and fill pair's lower third at a held moment, and a sound's aim after quick jumps. It then did what Session 50 left open: a video input as a playlist item and as a slide's background cue, by its name, and the picture-in-picture box's input checked after a restart; the layer bar's clears in the order of their keys, the View menu showing Cmd+1 and Cmd+2, and a letter with Cmd, Ctrl or Alt matching only with Shift as its key has it; Audience and Stage (a screen turned on again asks nothing, the question on an output over the controls and in the window's first moments, the top bar at 1024 and 1280 wide, the dashboard showing a switched-off screen as off); Keep these screens? asking for Shows and Sends, Go back putting back only what the change changed, a crash in the 15 seconds going back at the next start, and no dialog covering the question; the stage's audience picture on a node, with Audience off and on a tablet; Back from a video held at its end, the Music row, and recovery putting a video or sound back from its last jump; Swap moving the main picture, the stream's Camera layout and key and fill for picture in picture; HEVC, conversions kept over a quit, sounds from the drop folder and a converted original added again; and **Delete from the library…**. Kept as built: a video input's fixed one-second dissolve, Simple Mode never choosing an input in Media, Go back for the screens only, a test presentation only ever shown live removed at the update, and a tablet's previews under the remote's rules. New end-to-end specs: `small-window`, `function-keys`, `video-input-items`, `pip-input-recovery` and `stage-hall-picture`, with new or changed tests in `show-on`, `audience-stage`, `keep-screens`, `pip`, `recovery`, `skips`, `convert-at-import`, `drop-folder` and `placeholders`; how the checks came out: see the final report.

**Session 52** (23 Oct 2026; for 1.0.0-alpha.12, so the engine state goes to 13, as alpha.11 published 12, and reads 12 whole: `readsEngineVersion` in `src/shared/engine/state.ts`; the library stays at migration 47) did what five of Session 51's questions asked (2, 3, 4, 5 and 13), in six changes: a video input renamed in Media takes the library's playlist items and slides that named it to its new name; a slide's background whose file is missing leaves the background as it was and says so, as a missing video input does; Swap dissolves the main picture, with no black rectangle under it; the stage box says Audience off in its bottom left corner, and with no audience group it is black and says so; a screen's picture sent somewhere new by a skip or a marker lands in step as the sound does, and the first jump of a sound or a picture no longer overshoots; and a video on a slide, a prop's and the logo's play between their file's start and end points. The key and fill specs give their pair the main display and the test's extra one, as CI has (see "End-to-end tests on a computer someone is using"). New end-to-end spec: `missing-background-file`, with new or changed tests in `video-input-items`, `pip`, `stage-hall-picture`, `markers`, `prop-videos`, `recovery`, `keyfill` and `flash-pixels`; how the checks came out: see the final report.

Next (PLAN.md section 5.2): the mandir setup (`docs/setup-day.md`), where the importers meet the mandir's own files (the keys became the operators' own in Session 50), then the parallel run.

## The repository is public

`Priyansh9121/drashti` is public: everything pushed, and every CI log, can be read by anyone. So:

- **No secrets, no real content.** No keys, stream keys or passwords; no real kirtan or scripture text; nothing from the mandir's library or a computer's ProPresenter libraries. Tests and screenshots use placeholder text and generated media, and test output about real libraries is counts only. `.gitignore` blocks `.env`, `.mcp.json` and `migration-samples/` as a safety net.
- **Commits use the GitHub noreply address.**
- **Three checks catch a secret:** GitHub secret scanning with push protection (turned on 1 Oct 2026), a gitleaks scan of the whole history before each push (`.githooks/pre-push`; run `pnpm hooks` once in a new clone), and the same scan in CI. gitleaks has no allowlist: nothing in the history needs one.

## Download Drashti

Drashti is free. Each computer has one link, and it always gives the newest release (Session 18):

| Computer                               | Download                                                                                       |
| -------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| A Mac with Apple silicon (M1 or later) | https://github.com/Priyansh9121/drashti/releases/latest/download/Drashti-mac-apple-silicon.dmg |
| A Mac with an Intel processor          | https://github.com/Priyansh9121/drashti/releases/latest/download/Drashti-mac-intel.dmg         |
| A Windows 10 or 11 PC (64-bit)         | https://github.com/Priyansh9121/drashti/releases/latest/download/Drashti-windows-setup.exe     |

**Which Mac?** Apple menu, **About This Mac**: a **Chip** line (Apple M1, M2, M3 or later) means Apple silicon; a **Processor** line naming Intel means an Intel Mac. Drashti needs macOS 13 or later.

**Opening Drashti the first time, once on each computer** (Session 50). The installers are not signed: signing would need paid certificates (an Apple Developer ID, and a code-signing certificate for Windows), and Drashti uses nothing paid. **On a Mac**, open the `.dmg`: its window shows the two steps as a picture (see "Releases, signing and updates"). First, drag Drashti onto Applications. Then open it from Applications: the first time, macOS says Drashti can't be opened, as it could not verify it; press **Done**, then in **System Settings › Privacy & Security** press **Open Anyway** by the message about Drashti, and confirm. **Open Anyway** is there only after that first try to open it. For whoever looks after Drashti, one line in Terminal does the same, once Drashti is in Applications: `xattr -dr com.apple.quarantine /Applications/Drashti.app` (it takes away the mark macOS gives a download, so Drashti opens at once). **On Windows**, open the installer (if the browser says it is not commonly downloaded, keep it); at "Windows protected your PC" press **More info**, then **Run anyway**. The steps for the volunteers are in `docs/parallel-run.md` (section 2), for the admins in `docs/admin-guide.md` (section 2).

The links work: the first release was published on 9 Oct 2026 (by hand, from the Release workflow: "Releases, signing and updates"), and every release after it carries the same names. A release's files, with `SHA256SUMS.txt` to check a download by hand, are at https://github.com/Priyansh9121/drashti/releases/latest. Its two Mac `.zip` files are what Drashti's own update installs: nobody needs to download them.

## Setup

You need **Node.js 22.12 or later** and **pnpm 12**. Nothing else: better-sqlite3 ships prebuilt Node-API binaries, so no compiler is needed on either OS.

macOS (Terminal):

```bash
cd app
corepack enable pnpm        # or: npm install -g pnpm@12
pnpm install
```

Windows (PowerShell):

```powershell
cd app
corepack enable pnpm        # or: npm install -g pnpm@12
pnpm install
```

The Electron binary downloads the first time something needs it (for example `pnpm dev` or `pnpm test`).

Streaming and recording need the bundled **FFmpeg**, and YouTube in Import from a Link needs **yt-dlp** and **Deno**; none of them is ever in git. Fetch them once (each is checked against a pinned SHA-256, kept in `~/.cache/drashti/`, and put in `vendor/`):

```bash
node scripts/fetch-ffmpeg.mjs            # this computer's (add --target darwin-x64 to package Intel Macs too)
node scripts/fetch-youtube-tools.mjs     # yt-dlp and Deno, likewise (--target darwin-x64 too)
node scripts/check-ffmpeg.mjs            # Drashti finds them: FFmpeg speaks RTMPS, which encoders work here,
                                         # yt-dlp and Deno run, and Deno is sandboxed as yt-dlp starts it
```

## Commands

| Command            | What it does                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `pnpm dev`         | Run the app with hot reload.                                                                                                                                |
| `pnpm build`       | Build main, preload and renderer into `out/`.                                                                                                               |
| `pnpm test`        | Unit tests (Vitest), run inside Electron's own Node so they use the exact Node, V8 and native-module ABI the app ships with.                                |
| `pnpm test:e2e`    | Build, then run every Playwright test against the real app.                                                                                                 |
| `pnpm test:perf`   | Build, then run the performance check (by hand only; see "Performance check").                                                                              |
| `pnpm test:smoke`  | Build, then run only the smoke test: launch, go live on slide 1, check the output window shows it.                                                          |
| `pnpm lint`        | ESLint (type-aware) and a Prettier check.                                                                                                                   |
| `pnpm typecheck`   | TypeScript for the Node side, the web side and the end-to-end tests.                                                                                        |
| `pnpm format`      | Format everything with Prettier.                                                                                                                            |
| `pnpm test:audit`  | Tests for the audit kit in `tools/audit/`.                                                                                                                  |
| `pnpm secret-scan` | Scan the whole git history for secrets (gitleaks, findings redacted). Runs before every push and in CI.                                                     |
| `pnpm hooks`       | Turn on the git hooks in `.githooks/` for this clone (the secret scan before each push). Run once after cloning.                                            |
| `pnpm package`     | Build installers into `release/`: `.dmg` and `.zip` on macOS (Apple silicon and Intel), `.exe` on Windows, each with its FFmpeg. They are unsigned for now. |
| `pnpm icons`       | Make every icon file from Drashti's logo, `build/icon.svg` (on a Mac: Apple's `iconutil` makes the `.icns`). Commit what it makes.                          |

CI runs on GitHub Actions in the public `drashti` repository, where standard runners are free. `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs typecheck, lint and unit tests on Ubuntu for every push. Every push to `phase1` or `main`, pull requests and manual runs also get unit tests, the end-to-end tests (including the smoke test) and packaging on `macos-latest` and `windows-latest`, and keep the installers for 7 days. CI's computers are set light, so those two run the suite in White (Follow the computer); a third job, **End-to-end in Black (windows-latest)** (Session 41), runs it again on Windows with `DRASHTI_TEST_APPEARANCE=black`, with no unit tests or packaging, on the same runs (not a quick check or a Mac-only run). A push cancels the run still going on the same branch, so the last push's run is the one that counts. A manual run can pick one OS:

```bash
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=windows   # or os=macos, os=both
```

A manual run can also be a quick check: only the specs it names, with no unit tests on the Mac or Windows runner and no packaging. Each manual run has its own concurrency group, so pushes and manual runs never cancel each other.

```bash
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=windows -f specs="tests/e2e/operator-safety.spec.ts"
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=macos -f specs="tests/e2e/remote.spec.ts" -f repeat=5   # each test 5 times
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=macos -f specs="tests/e2e/network.spec.ts" -f quiet=true
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=macos -f screenshots=true   # kept as the screenshots-macOS artifact
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=windows -f unit="src/main/stream/worker/pipeline.test.ts" -f repeat=40   # unit tests, 40 times (Session 16)
gh workflow run CI --ref <branch> -f os=both -f pixels_base=<full sha>   # the audience screens against an earlier commit (Session 26g)
```

- `pixels_base` (Session 26g) builds the earlier commit in a worktree and runs `tests/e2e/audience-pixels.spec.ts` on both builds: the hall's output, a stage screen, the stream's picture, the stage display on a tablet and the display numbers, through the seeded placeholder slides, a generated picture, a stage message and black-out. Every picture must match within 2 per channel (the stage clock is masked). Locally: `pnpm build && node scripts/audience-pixels.mjs <commit>`.

- `unit` (Session 16) runs only the unit test files it names, `repeat` times each, and says how many runs failed; with no specs it runs no end-to-end tests.
- **The two checks that failed now and then** (Session 16). The slow end of the output paint check (`tests/e2e/operator.spec.ts`: the 12th of 15 slide changes painted within 34 ms) failed about 1 attempt in 25 on CI Macs. 26 attempts on CI Macs showed why: its first three measured changes were the output's first paints of those slides, 11 of 78 over 34 ms against 8 of 312 for the rest. The test now puts every slide up once, unmeasured, before it measures; the budgets are as they were. 26 attempts after: the first three changes 0 of 78 over 34 ms, all of them 3 of 390, the 12th of 15 at worst 28 ms. One attempt in each set of 26 failed the median instead (24 ms, then 18 ms, against 17): each time 8 of its 15 changes took over 17 ms (up to 39 and 67 ms) among fast ones (2 to 13 ms), a run of late frames on the virtual machine, which the test cannot tell from a slow Drashti. That part is left for Priyansh to decide (the session report has the numbers). The FFmpeg stream test (`src/main/stream/worker/pipeline.test.ts`), which failed once on Windows before `9561baa`, passed 40 runs of 40 on Windows after it (160 tests).
- `quiet=true` runs the specs in the quiet test mode on a macOS runner while `scripts/quiet-check.mjs` watches the front app. It then runs them once more as an operator would, where Drashti must be seen in front (see "End-to-end tests on a computer someone is using").

**No .only, no flaky tests** (Sessions 31–36, TST-3). On CI, `playwright.config.ts` sets `forbidOnly` (a `.only` left in fails the run) and `failOnFlakyTests` (a test that passes only on its retry makes the run red). Retries stay at 1, so a failure's first attempt keeps its trace and screenshots; a flaky test is then fixed, or, if the virtual machine stalled, its run is started once more and the report names it. Shown on scratch branches: a planted `.only` stopped the run before any test ran, and a planted test that passed only on its retry turned it red.

**The bar guard** (Sessions 31–36, TST-8). CI's checks job runs `scripts/bar-guard.mjs` against `main` and writes, in the job summary, every line of the change that lowers a bar the tests hold: a time limit raised, a budget loosened (the paint, key and fill, node and main-process budgets in `operator.spec.ts`, `keyfill.spec.ts`, `nodes-show.spec.ts`, `src/main/perftest.ts` and the accessibility rule in `tests/e2e/a11y.ts` are watched by name), a test skipped, a lint rule turned off. It also counts the ratchets, which should only ever go down (lint rules turned off, skipped tests, raw waits in end-to-end tests, type checks turned off), against `main`. It reports; it does not fail CI yet.

**Races** (Sessions 31–36). Tests that pin what happens when two things meet: two remotes pressing Next at once move one slide (`tests/e2e/remote.spec.ts`; a phone's or the API's Next carries the revision it saw, and one the show has moved past changes nothing); a node whose link drops part-way through a dissolve, either way round, ends on Main's slide (`tests/e2e/nodes-show.spec.ts`, the node's link dropped through `drashtiNode.dropLink()`); after a crash the first action lands on what recovery put back, since recovery runs before the engine takes any command (`tests/e2e/recovery.spec.ts`); and the slide editor saves only over what it opened, whichever change comes first and even within one millisecond (`src/main/library/slides-ipc.test.ts`, with a content revision in its stamp).

**When a test fails on CI** (flake triage, Sessions 31–36, TST-9). A run is red when any test fails, or passes only on its retry (`failOnFlakyTests`). Then:

1. Read the first attempt: its error, trace and screenshots are kept (the retry is only there to tell a flake from a failure). The run's `playwright-report-<system>` artifact has them; delete it once read.
2. A real failure, or a flake that comes from Drashti or the test (a race, a wait for a while instead of a condition, a measurement taken at the wrong moment): fix it, red first, with the fix's own evidence. Never raise a time limit or a budget, add a retry, or skip the test to make it pass: the bar guard says so in the job summary.
3. A virtual machine that stalled (a frame held back for seconds, a decoder falling behind, an install that lost its connection), seen in the first attempt's figures: the run gets one re-run, and the report names it. A second failure of the same test is not a stall.
4. Each flake and what was done goes in the session's report; the named ones stay listed until fixed.

The named ones (Sessions 31–36):

- `sound.spec.ts` and `markers.spec.ts`, the sound coming into step: **fixed.** A loop went round by the element's own loop, which came round late (a sound 0.2 to 0.3 s), so a looping video's sound fell further behind its picture each time round; now a loop goes round by an aimed jump, and a file still out of step once it plays on is aimed again (`src/renderer/src/render/playback.ts`).
- `operator.spec.ts`' paint median (a slide change painted within 17 ms, the median of 15): **the cause is CI's Mac,** which misses frames. Each change's time splits, in the test's log, into its message and render (0 to 13 ms on CI's Mac) and its wait for the next frame. In ten runs and their two retries (run 39020856129) the Mac's frames came 16.7 ms apart at the median, but every run went 33 ms or more between two frames at least once (a frame missed; twice a stall of 94 and 144 ms), and a change that came just before a missed frame waited up to 32 ms for the next: a few such among the fifteen took the median to 18 in one run. Windows (run 39020858834, ten runs) drew its frames 15.6 ms apart, 15.8 at most, and no change waited more than 15 ms. The budget stays; a Mac run that fails it with the frames' log showing a missed frame is a stalled machine (3 above).
- `markers.spec.ts`, a file played once stopping at its end point: checked. In every run kept this session (84 copies' figures) the furthest a copy played was 2.158 s for an end point at 2.137 s, 21 ms past it, inside the two frames (33 ms) allowed.

Session 50's release gate (run 39794606286) met three more, each **fixed** in Session 51 by mending Drashti; the tests were right:

- `show-on.spec.ts:81` (now `:84`), on all three jobs, both tries. With an empty library the middle's Start here centred its three steps in a place that could neither shrink nor scroll, so in CI's window, about 1024 × 645 (its displays are 1024 wide), they ran under the layer bar and the status bar, and step 3's **Show on** could not be clicked (the status bar took the click). The middle's empty places (Start here, a playlist item's note, a video input item's view, a presentation with no slides yet, a presentation that could not be opened) are now centred while they fit and scroll from their top when they do not (`CentredPlace` in `ui/States.tsx`), the open playlist's and the slides' "What is this?" shrink and scroll in their places, and the layer bar, which scrolled sideways at 1024 wide, fits (see the keymap's paragraph, `src/shared/keymap.ts`, under "Running a show"). The spec now sizes the window as CI's wherever it runs (`tests/e2e/window-size.ts`), and `tests/e2e/small-window.spec.ts` checks Start here at 1024 × 645 and 1024 × 768 in an empty library, a video input item's view and those two cards at both sizes, the welcome and the practice show at 1024 × 645, the layer bar from 1008 to 1920 px wide in every state, and Simple Mode's big buttons with their keys.
- `lower-thirds.spec.ts:170` (a key and fill pair's moving lower third alike at every held moment), on the Mac both tries, and flaky on Windows. A lower third ended its in, change or out only in its window's next animation frame, and any other drawing painted the old phase; Session 50's picture in picture drew the whole Scene again at every clock jump, so at a moment held past a motion's end a window with no frame yet drew the lower third still coming in (or going, at no opacity) while the other, a frame ahead, drew it still: the key a step behind the fill. Now every drawing works out what to draw from the engine's time (`lowerThirdAt` and `nextLowerThirdRun` in `render/lower-third-motion.ts`; the Lower Thirds panel's lower third by its own `runFor`, in `render/LowerThirdView.tsx`, the same way), draws again at a clock jump by its own listener, and the frame in which a motion ends draws it still or gone at once; the picture-in-picture box is worked out the same way, and a clock jump draws the Scene again only when the box changed.
- `skips.spec.ts:193` (a sound's Back 15 s, Forward 15 s and Last 10 s), flaky on Windows. CI pressed each skip about 0.45 s after the last, before the audio player had measured that landing; the check the skip set off measured it anyway, from the new place (15 or 30 s off), so the lead it learned went to its 0.5 s cap and Forward 15 s landed 0.4 s ahead, and with its re-aims used up just before, it was eased back at 2% a second, still out of step when the test's 15 s ran out. A sound the engine sends somewhere new now measures nothing from before the jump and has re-aims of its own (`render/playback.ts`; see "Sound" below). Session 52 did the same for the screens' pictures, which after quick skips could land up to 0.45 s ahead, or a seek's time behind.

**Traces of Drashti's windows when a test fails** (TST-4) were tried and dropped (Sessions 31–36): kept, they must add under 10% to the end-to-end time, and no form of them came near. A Playwright trace of every window in every test, run beside the same commit without one, both systems at once (runs 39021815038 and 39021823995, then 39025957046 and 39025965406):

| Traces                         | Mac                          | Windows                      | What else they did                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------ | ---------------------------- | ---------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Screenshots and page snapshots | 54.4 against 46.7 min (+16%) | 67.5 against 37.0 min (+82%) | three failures and two flaky tests where timing is tested (the warm-up, a reloaded screen, the setup wizard, the guides, the sound); 1.3 and 1.6 GB of traces |
| Page snapshots only            | 64.4 against 54.0 min (+19%) | 55.9 against 38.0 min (+47%) | a flaky test on each system; 320 MB of traces each                                                                                                            |

A failed test's first attempt keeps its error and a screenshot of each of its windows (Playwright takes them on a failure), as before.

A **secret scan** job runs on every push and pull request: `scripts/secret-scan.mjs` downloads one pinned gitleaks release (its sha256 is written in the script and checked before use), proves on a throwaway repository that a planted token is found and printed redacted, then scans every commit of every branch with `--redact` and fails the run on any finding. The end-to-end jobs wait for it.

Each unit or end-to-end run keeps its temporary files in one folder, `drashti-run-<process id>-…` in the system's temporary folder (`scripts/temp-root.mjs`; TMPDIR, TEMP and TMP point into it for the tests and the apps they start), and removes it at the end. A run that crashed leaves its folder behind; the next run clears it, and any loose `drashti-*` folders over an hour old.

`.github/workflows/release.yml` builds a release (Session 14; see "Releases, signing and updates"): started by hand, it publishes only from `main` with **publish** ticked; otherwise, and on a push to `release-dry-run`, it is a dry run that keeps the files as an artifact.

`.github/workflows/audit-kit.yml` tests the audit scripts on both OSes, including under Windows PowerShell 5.1, when they change on `main` or in a pull request, or when started by hand.

### End-to-end tests on a computer someone is using

Each end-to-end test starts Drashti, and Drashti running as an operator would takes the keyboard and puts outputs over the whole screen. So the full suite runs on CI. On a computer someone is using, run one spec at a time (never with `--repeat-each`). Runs outside CI are quiet: the test helpers set `DRASHTI_TEST_QUIET=1` (`src/main/windows/quiet.ts`), and a packaged Drashti ignores it.

- **Drashti never becomes the active app.** On macOS it runs as an accessory app, with no Dock icon and no menu bar. Every window is shown without activating it, and nothing focuses, raises or full-screens a window.
- **Nothing covers the screen.** Outputs open as ordinary windows, and every window is see-through and lets clicks through to whatever is under it. The pages still draw as on a real screen, and Playwright stands in for keyboard focus.
- **It makes no sound.** A system dialog that no test answered is answered with its cancel button instead of appearing.

Some tests need real focus, a real full-screen output or a whole display. They skip themselves in quiet runs and run on CI:

- covering and uncovering the operator's display;
- the screens test's full-screen output;
- the setup wizard's cover step;
- the watchdog self-test;
- the screenshots;
- the performance check.

`DRASHTI_E2E_LOUD=1` runs them locally as CI does, but only when whoever is at the computer agrees.

**A computer with more displays than CI's** (Session 52). `keyfill.spec.ts` and `flash-pixels.spec.ts` give their key and fill pair the main display and the test's extra one (`DRASHTI_EXTRA_DISPLAYS=1`, a pretend copy of the main one, with windowed outputs), CI's two, however many displays this computer has, so the fill's and the key's windows are on one physical screen and their pixels read alike. They gave the pair every display: with a second real display that made three screens, and with only the first two the key's window sat on the other physical screen, whose colours read 1.4 to 2% apart mid-transition.

To check quiet runs on macOS, run `node scripts/quiet-check.mjs <spec>` after `pnpm build`. It runs the spec while asking `lsappinfo front` every half second which app is in front, and fails if Drashti or Electron ever is.

- `--expect-front` with `DRASHTI_E2E_LOUD=1` is the control run: Drashti must be seen in front there, or the check can't see it.
- `DRASHTI_E2E_QUIET=1` makes a CI run quiet as well.

**When Drashti crashes in a test**, the test's output says so with the exit code (on Windows, `0xffff7003` is a crash with no crash handler to write a dump). A window that crashed is in Drashti's log with how its process ended (Session 39): `Watchdog: operator crashed (crashed, exit code 0xC0000005)` on Windows, `(crashed, signal 11: SIGSEGV)` or a plain number on a Mac. Since Session 39 an end-to-end test's Drashti writes its crash files into that test's own folder under `test-results/` (`crash-files/`; `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` sets `DRASHTI_TEST_CRASH_DUMPS`), and Playwright keeps a test's folder only when it fails (`preserveOutput` in `playwright.config.ts`), so a failed CI run's `playwright-report-*` artifact carries the failed tests' crash files, and a green run's carries none (the deliberate crashes in passing tests, such as the watchdog self-test and `crash-files.spec.ts`, leave nothing). CI keeps the earliest 200 MB of them and lists the rest in `test-results/crash-files-left-out.txt` (`scripts/cap-crash-files.mjs`). The artifacts are public and a crash file holds the process's memory, its environment too: the end-to-end job's environment holds no secret (no token is passed to it, and the checkout keeps no credentials; checked in Session 39 by listing its variables' names in scratch run 39202814696: the only password-like name was `PGPASSWORD`, which GitHub's Windows image sets for its own PostgreSQL, a published default and nothing of this repository's), so keep it that way. Outside CI, run a spec with `DRASHTI_TEST_CRASH_DUMPS` pointing at a folder of your own. Read them with `minidump_stackwalk` and Electron's symbols for that version and platform, which `npx electron-minidump` fetches from symbols.electronjs.org. A dump holds the test app's memory, so keep it out of public places once read. This is how the Windows Uncover crash in Session 10 was found (see `src/main/outputs/electron-outputs.ts`).

**A crash as Drashti quits fails the test** (Session 37): closing it (`app.close()` in `tests/e2e/helpers.ts`) fails when its process ends on a signal or a crash's exit code, except for a Drashti stopped dead on purpose. Quit a Drashti a test has just started only once it has started (its operator window ready, the audio player made): Playwright's quit runs in the middle of whatever the main process is doing, and Electron 44 itself crashes (SIGSEGV, in `BaseWindow::WillCloseWindow` as it says "close" to a window whose JavaScript side is not made yet) when a quit comes inside the making of a window. A ten-line Electron script that quits from `web-contents-created` crashes the same way, so it is Electron's, and a person's quit never comes inside a window's making. `quit.spec.ts`'s stopped-dead test quit that early, so its restarted Drashti ended on SIGSEGV on CI's Mac now and then (2 of the 4 full runs before Session 37, 4 of 6 tries in its scratch runs) while the test passed; every dump read, CI's and the dev Mac's, was that.

### Switches

**Which a packaged Drashti honours** (Session 30, `src/main/switches.ts`): the few the guides give admins, marked **Packaged too** below, and the performance check's (`DRASHTI_PERF_*`, windowed outputs, extra displays) only while `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=performance` runs. It ignores every other switch, every `DRASHTI_TEST_*`, `DRASHTI_FFMPEG` and `DRASHTI_UPDATE_URL` among them: those are for development and the tests. Every switch is read through that one place, and a unit test reads the source to keep it so.

| Environment variable                       | Effect                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `DRASHTI_USER_DATA_DIR=<dir>`              | **Packaged too.** Use this folder for the library and settings (tests use a fresh one each run).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `DRASHTI_WINDOWED_OUTPUTS=1`               | **Packaged: the performance check only.** Development only: outputs open as normal windows, to try them on a computer with one screen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DRASHTI_EXTRA_DISPLAYS=<n>`               | **Packaged: the performance check only.** With windowed outputs: up to 4 pretend displays (copies of the main one), to try several outputs on one screen.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_DIAGNOSTICS=1`                    | **Packaged too.** Adds a Diagnostics menu with the watchdog self-test and crash buttons (on a node too, since Session 17).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=watchdog`                | **Packaged too.** Runs the watchdog self-test headless, prints the result and exits (used by the tests).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=node-watchdog`           | **Packaged too.** A node's own watchdog self-test, headless, with the node's data folder (its pairing): prints the result and exits (Session 17; used by the tests).                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=performance`             | **Packaged too.** Runs the performance check headless in a throwaway library, prints the result and exits (see "Performance check").                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_PRIORITY=normal`                  | **Packaged too.** Windows: this run at normal priority (or `above-normal`), whatever File > Run Ahead of Other Programs says (Session 16); the performance check compares the two with it.                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=restore-relaunch`        | **Packaged too.** Backs up, asks for a restore and restarts for real, then the new copy checks the library and writes `result.json` into `DRASHTI_SELFTEST_DIR` (used by `scripts/check-relaunch.mjs`).                                                                                                                                                               |
| `DRASHTI_NO_QUIT_CONFIRM=1`                | **Packaged too.** Skips the "Quit Drashti?" question (used by the tests).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_LOG_PERMISSIONS=1`                | **Packaged too.** Logs every permission a page checks or asks for (to see why a sound output cannot be chosen).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_MEDIA_DELAY_MS=<ms>`         | Tests only: media answers this late (up to 5 s), as from a slow disk.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_RELAUNCH=1`               | Tests only: after Restore Library…, quit instead of restarting (the test starts Drashti again itself).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_SINGLE_KEYS_UNREADABLE=1`    | Tests only: reading the single-letter keys setting fails, as a refused read of the library would (Session 38): the operator window shows with them on, and the log says why.                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_UNREADABLE=<reads>`          | Tests only: the start-up reads named (`playlists`, `playlist-items`, `presentation`, `engine`, comma-separated) fail until the test calls `globalThis.drashtiTestReadable(name)` in the main process (Session 39): the window says what it could not read, with Try again, and the log names it once.                                                                 |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_APPEARANCE=<id>`             | Tests only: start in this appearance (`black`, `white`, `saffron`, … or `follow`), whatever was kept: the guides’ pictures in Black (`screenshots.spec.ts`, Sessions 39–40) and CI's Black job (Session 41). Empty: none, as tests of Follow the computer, the default and a kept choice give it (`OWN_APPEARANCE` in `tests/e2e/helpers.ts`).                        |
| `DRASHTI_SELFTEST=ffmpeg`                  | **Packaged too.** Checks the bundled FFmpeg (found, version, RTMPS, which H.264 encoders work), yt-dlp and Deno (found, version, and Deno started as yt-dlp starts it can read no file, reach no address and start no program), prints the result and exits (used by `scripts/check-ffmpeg.mjs`).                                                                     |
| `DRASHTI_FFMPEG=<path>`                    | Use this FFmpeg instead of the bundled one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_FAKE_DEVICES=1`              | Tests only: Chromium's fake camera (a moving test pattern) and fake microphone (a beep) stand in for real ones (since Session 50 as a video input too, "fake_device_0"), and the system is not asked for camera or microphone access.                                                                                                                                 |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_SAFE_STORAGE=1`           | Tests only: behave as if the system's secure storage were missing, so no stream key can be saved.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CRASH_DUMPS=<dir>`           | Tests only: crash dumps (minidumps) go to this folder instead of the data folder's `Crashpad`, never sent anywhere (see "End-to-end tests on a computer someone is using").                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_QUIET=1`                     | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the quiet test mode (see "End-to-end tests on a computer someone is using").                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| `DRASHTI_ROLE=main` or `node`              | **Packaged too.** Start as Main or as a node (kept in the data folder for later starts). With a `DRASHTI_USER_DATA_DIR` of its own, a node can run beside Main on one computer.                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| `DRASHTI_NODE_PORT=<port>`                 | **Packaged too.** The port Main listens for nodes on (8741 unless given).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_VERSION=<version>`           | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the version this copy says it runs, to see Main and a node on different versions refuse each other.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CLOCK_SKEW_MS=<ms>`          | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a node's clock as if it were this far off, to see it measure Main's clock and correct for it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_COMPUTER_NAME=<name>`        | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the computer's name as Main and its nodes show it (screenshots never show a test computer's own).                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_COPY_RATE=<bytes>`           | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Main copies media to nodes at this rate (bytes a second, 1024 at least), so a test can watch a copy under way.                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_ARTI_CLOCK=1`                | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the schedules' clock (scheduled backups; the arti's and macros' too, until Session 44) can be moved by a test (`globalThis.drashtiArtiClock(wallMs)`), starting `DRASHTI_TEST_ARTI_OFFSET_MS` ahead. The engine's clock never moves.                                                                                       |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_BACKUP_RATE=<bytes>`         | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: scheduled backups copy at this rate (bytes a second).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| `DRASHTI_UPDATE_URL=<url>`                 | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: look for updates at this server (GitHub Releases' paths) instead of GitHub.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_UPDATE_INSTALL=<file>`       | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: installing an update at quit writes what it would run into this file, and runs nothing.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_UPDATE_RATE=<bytes>`         | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: updates download at this rate (bytes a second).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_RENDER_ERRORS=1`             | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a test can make an output draw an element that throws (`globalThis.drashtiTestRenderError(screenId?)`), to see the error caught, logged and the output reloaded.                                                                                                                                                           |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_DISPLAY_CHANGES=1`           | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: with pretend displays, a test can make them come and go while Drashti runs (`globalThis.drashtiTestDisplays(n)`), as a projector that drops for a moment does.                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_GIVE_UP=1`                   | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a test can mark an output as given up on (`globalThis.drashtiTestGiveUp(screenId)`), as a crash loop would, to see Stopped and Try again.                                                                                                                                                                                  |
