| `DRASHTI_TEST_ON_AIR_HOOK=1`               | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a test can say the stream is on air (`globalThis.drashtiTestOnAir(true)`), for updates, link downloads, smart playlists and the drop folder (Session 28), and since Session 50 conversions, which wait while it is.                                                                                                        |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_LINK_ORIGIN=<url>`           | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Import from a Link sends every request to this local stand-in for Dropbox instead (`http://127.0.0.1:<port>`; `tests/e2e/dropbox-stand-in.ts`), with the address it stands for in an `x-drashti-test-host` header.                                                                                                         |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_LINK_GUARD=1`                | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti, and set for every e2e run (`tests/global-setup.ts`): the download process refuses any request to anywhere but `127.0.0.1`, so no test can reach Dropbox or YouTube; the stream goes to no place but `127.0.0.1` (Session 28), and events out to no address but `127.0.0.1`, each refused loudly in the log (`TEST GUARD`). |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_FAKE_YT_DLP=<file>`          | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: YouTube in Import from a Link starts this script (`tests/e2e/fake-yt-dlp.cjs`, run by Electron as Node) instead of yt-dlp; `DRASHTI_TEST_FAKE_YT_DLP_CONFIG` (JSON) tells it what to answer. With any `DRASHTI_TEST_` switch on and no fake, YouTube is off: tests never start the real yt-dlp.                            |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_DOWNLOADS_DIR=<dir>`         | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Import from a Link's first folder is "Drashti downloads" in this folder, not in Movies or Videos.                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_CONVERTER=1`              | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: imports act as if neither Keynote nor PowerPoint were installed (PowerPoint and Keynote files are reported, not converted).                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_DECK_CONVERTER=1`            | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a stand-in saves PowerPoint files as PDF in place of Keynote and PowerPoint (`src/main/import/stand-in-converter.ts`: one plain page per slide shown), so a test on a computer with neither can make decks (Session 26a).                                                                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CONVERTERS=1`                | Unit tests on a Mac only, never on CI: `src/main/import/deck-converters.test.ts` drives the real Keynote and PowerPoint with placeholder decks (quit both first; it starts and quits them).                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CLOCK_HOLD=1`                | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a test can hold the screens' clock at one moment (`globalThis.drashtiTestHoldClock(at)`), on Main's outputs, the stream's page and a node's, to compare them part-way through a transition (Sessions 26d and 27).                                                                                                          |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_HOLD_WARM_UP=1`              | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: every page keeps the GPU's warm-up drawn (Session 27, "Warming the GPU" in `docs/design.md`), for a test to look at; the show is never drawn.                                                                                                                                                                              |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_SOUND_MS=<ms>`            | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: No sound (Session 31) shows after this much quiet on air or recording, not 20 s.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_RECORD_PART_BYTES=<bytes>`   | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: a recording's part holds at most this many bytes, not 3.5 GB (Session 31).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_GUIDES_DELAY_MS=<ms>`        | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: each guide is read this much later, so a test sees Loading wait its half second (Sessions 31–36).                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_WINDOW_STANDIN=1`            | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: windows titled "Placeholder…" that a test opens in Drashti's own process stand in for other apps' windows in Show a Window… (Session 48), so no test needs Screen Recording or sees the computer's own windows; `no-access` stands in for a Mac without Screen Recording.                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_PERF_ZOOM=1`                      | **Packaged: the performance check only.** The streaming check opens the window for Zoom before it goes live (Session 48).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `DRASHTI_PERF_NO_WINDOW=1`                 | **Packaged: the performance check only.** The `live-window` scenario with no window put up, to compare (Session 48).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CLOCK_STYLE=<locale> <zone>` | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Main writes the time in this locale and time zone ("en-GB Pacific/Chatham"), so a test sees a node show Main's time, not its own (Sessions 31–36).                                                                                                                                                                         |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_SEED_PLACEHOLDERS=1`         | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti, and never for a self-test: a new library gets the placeholder presentations ("Language test slides", "Sample kirtan (placeholder)") it no longer has (Session 50). `launchApp` in `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` sets it; `NO_PLACEHOLDERS` there (it empty) starts a test as an operator's new library starts.                    |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_KEEP_SCREENS=0`              | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Keep these screens? (Session 50) is never asked, so a change to the screens is kept at once. `launchApp` sets '0'; a test about the question sets '1' (`tests/e2e/keep-screens.spec.ts`).                                                                                                                                  |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_KEEP_SCREENS_MS=<ms>`        | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: Keep these screens? goes back by itself after this long, not 15 s (Session 50).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_CAMERA_ACCESS=<word>`        | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the camera's system setting stands as this for video inputs (Session 50): `granted`, `denied`, `not-determined`, `restricted` or `unknown`. Nothing asks macOS (asked, `not-determined` becomes `granted`) or opens System Settings: the log says what would open.                                                         |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_CONVERT_AT_IMPORT=1`      | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: an import judges media by their headers alone, as before Session 50, and converts nothing by itself, so a test can press Convert as an operator does (`tests/e2e/convert.spec.ts`).                                                                                                                                        |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_SCREENS_LATE_MS=<ms>`        | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: the operator window's first ask for the screens is answered this much later (Session 51), so a test can press Cmd+1 in the window's first moments, before it knows which screens are open (`tests/e2e/audience-stage.spec.ts`).                                                                                            |
| `DRASHTI_TEST_PLAYS_HEVC=1` or `0`         | Tests only, ignored by a packaged Drashti: whether this computer plays HEVC, given rather than asked of the operator window (Session 51), as no test can count on a real HEVC decoder: `0` and an import converts HEVC by itself, `1` and it plays as it is (`tests/e2e/convert-at-import.spec.ts`).                                                                  |

If you start Drashti from inside another Electron app's process (for example an editor extension), make sure `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE` is not set in that environment. When it's set, Electron starts as plain Node. The end-to-end tests clear it automatically.

## Running a show

**Another app's window as the background** (Session 48, `src/main/live-window/`, `src/renderer/src/live-window/`). **Show a Window…** in the library's Media tab (Pro Mode) lists other apps' open windows, by name with a small live picture: the main process lists only windows (`desktopCapturer`, types `window`), and leaves out any display and every one of Drashti's own (`sources.ts`); only a window it just offered can go up, through `ShowEngine.showWindow`, never an engine command (engine state 12: a background of kind `window`). Every screen's page on this computer (each output, the stream's page, the operator window's pictures, small) captures it once (`render/live-window.ts`) after asking the main process, whose capture handler then gives that page that window, once: no page names what it captures. It is fitted inside with black around it and silent; slides and everything above go over it, black-out and the logo cover it, Clear all and F3 take it off, Put it back brings it back. Its picture is kept about once a second: when the window closes or hides, the screens keep the last one and a notice in the window's flow says so (the main process looks every 2 s), with Clear the window. Stage screens say "Window on the screens", phones name it, nodes draw nothing for it, and restart recovery names it but never puts it back. On a Mac it needs Screen Recording (System Settings; ad hoc signing can make macOS ask again after an update). The tests use stand-in placeholder windows (`DRASHTI_TEST_WINDOW_STANDIN`): `tests/e2e/show-a-window.spec.ts`.

**A video input as the background** (Session 50, `src/main/video-inputs/`, `src/renderer/src/video-inputs/`, `render/video-input.ts`). **Add a Video Input…**, beside Show a Window… in the library's Media tab (Pro Mode), lists this computer's cameras and capture inputs by name with small live pictures (a capture card, or a video mixer's program through its USB output); the operator chooses one and names it (it offers "Input 1"; a name another input has is refused; 60 characters at most, and 20 inputs on a computer), with **Fit inside, with black around it** or **Fill the screen**, and **Add to Media** keeps it: nothing goes up from the dialog. Media's **Video inputs**, above the files, lists them, each with its own small live picture and a line saying how it sits or why it has no picture; one click puts one up through the engine's `showInput`, which looks it up in this computer's list by its id (a page never names a device): a background of kind `input` (engine state 12) that dissolves in over a fixed second (`INPUT_FADE_MS`, as the mandir's setup does), with the slide, the lower third, messages and the ticker over it. **Change** and **Delete** (asked first) sit at the row's end; the input up now cannot be deleted until it is cleared. The list is kept in `drashti-video-inputs.json` in the data folder, never in the library (a device's name belongs to its computer, and a backup restored on another must never name devices it does not have); naming, changing and deleting them is an admin's with roles on. Each page that draws it on this computer (every output, the stream's page, LIVE and Media's small pictures) opens it once, by the device's name, video only and never a microphone; black-out and the logo cover it, Clear all and F3 take it off, Put it back brings it back. It shows on LIVE, the hall's screens and the stream's Slides layout (YouTube sees what the hall sees); the Camera layout keeps the stream's own camera; stage screens say "Video input on the screens"; a phone names it ("Video input: Input 1") with Clear, and can never choose one in Media (a playlist's video input item it may start, below); nodes draw nothing for it, and the screens dashboard says so. No sound comes from it in the hall; a mixer's USB output can be the stream's sound input instead. When it stops (unplugged, or another app has it) the screens keep its last picture (`render/KeptVideo.tsx`, copied about once a second), a notice in the window's flow says so with **Clear the input**, and it comes back by itself with the input. After a restart it is put back while that input is in this computer's list; when the screens find no camera of that name (since Session 51 they have up to 20 s to say, not 8: `RESTORE_WAIT_MS`, as a screen's first frame after a crash took up to 8.1 s on CI's Mac), it comes off and the recovery notice says "The video input “Input 1” was not there after the restart, so it was not put back." On a Mac the camera is asked for only after Drashti has said why (**Continue**), and no page is given a camera until System Settings' Camera says allowed; a refusal shows the steps with **Open System Settings** (Privacy & Security › Camera). Windows says plainly when its camera privacy setting is off, with **Open Settings**. Simple Mode cannot choose one in Media, and can clear it. **In a playlist, and behind a slide** (Session 51, `inputNamed` in `src/shared/video-inputs.ts`): a row of Media's Video inputs goes into a playlist as the library's other rows do (**Add to playlist** under the chosen row, **Add to “…”** in its right-click menu, or dragged into the playlist), and the slide editor's picker for a slide's background lists **Video inputs on this computer**. Both keep the input's own name ("Input 1"), never its id: an input belongs to one computer while a library travels (a backup put back on another, a spare computer taking over), so each computer finds its own input of that name, in any letter case or Unicode form. Migration 47 builds `playlist_items` again with the kind `input` and an `input_name` column (every row and rowid kept, its indexes and the nodes' triggers made again); a slide's cue is a background cue with no file and `{"input": "Input 1"}` in its settings. The item's card says **Video input**, the middle shows its live picture, and a click or Next puts it up as the background over its fixed second and takes the slide off, as a picture or video item does; a slide whose cue names one puts that input up behind the slide, and the same input carries on. On a computer with no input of that name it is as a missing file is: the card says **Not on this computer**, the middle says why ("No video input called “Input 1” is on this computer, so Next goes past it."), starting it says "The video input “Input 1” is not on this computer", Next goes on past it, and a slide naming it leaves the background as it was, never black because of it, while LIVE says "Video input not on this computer: “Input 1”. The background stays as it was." **Renamed** (Session 52, `carryInputName` in `src/main/db/input-renames.ts`): renaming an input in Media (its row's **Change**, its **Name**, **Save**) carries every playlist item and slide's background cue in the library that named it to the new name, in one write, before anyone is told (`VideoInputsService.update`, through its `renamed`): removed items, templates and playlists in the bin too, and the slides' copies kept for Undo (`Revisions.carryInput`), each name matched as `inputNamed` matches it (`namesInput`: any letter case or Unicode form, spaces at either end aside); a picture's or a video's cue, and a header with the same words, are left alone. What is up carries on (it follows the input by its id), Next finds it by the new name, and the phones and nodes see the playlists change. It is not on the library's Undo: renaming it back carries them back. When the library cannot be written the rename is refused and the input keeps its name ("The playlists and slides that name “Input 1” could not be changed, so it keeps its name. Try again."). Another computer still finds its own input by the name, so give a spare computer's input the same new name there (its own **Change**), or its item and slide say Not on this computer; a backup made before the rename still names the old one. Before, a rename left every item and slide that named the input on the old name, Not on this computer (`tests/e2e/video-input-items.spec.ts`; `src/main/db/input-renames.test.ts`). A slide's thumbnail names the input behind it, marked where this computer has none of that name; Next's preview, the stage screens and a phone name it ("Video input: Input 1"; a phone may start the item, as any item), templates keep it, Repeat Slides refuses it, restart recovery keeps the playlist's place on it, and Simple Mode plays it through the playlist (its Next, or a click on the item), still never choosing one in Media (`tests/e2e/video-input-items.spec.ts`). If the mixer's program is Drashti's own picture, showing it makes a picture inside itself, over and over: put it up while the mixer shows a camera (the dialog says so). The tests use Chromium's fake camera, named "Placeholder input" (`DRASHTI_TEST_FAKE_DEVICES`, `DRASHTI_TEST_CAMERA_ACCESS`): `tests/e2e/video-input.spec.ts`.

**Picture in picture** (Session 50, `src/main/engine/pip.ts`, `src/main/pip/`, `render/PipLayerView.tsx`, `render/pip.ts`). **Picture in Picture…**, beside Show a Window… and Add a Video Input… in the library's Media tab (Pro Mode), chooses what goes in a box on the audience screens (the slides, a video input, a picture or video from Media, or a window Show a Window… offers), its corner, its size (**Small**, **Medium** or **Large**: a quarter, a third or two fifths of the screen's width, always 16:9), a thin border, rounded corners, and how it comes on and goes off (a fade, a scale, or a slide in from its corner); nothing goes up until **Show in the box**. The engine's `layers.pip` holds it, its picture looked up by the main process and never taken from a page, and every screen works out its in, out, swap and move from the engine's clock, at every drawing since Session 51 (only opacity and transform move, as the lower thirds do; a clock jump draws the screen again only when the box changed). With the slides in it, the main picture shows the background, props, messages and the ticker without the slide, and **Swap** shows the slide full with the background in the box; with anything else in it, Swap puts it in the background's place and the background in the box. Since Session 52 the one picture that changes on the main picture (the box's own, or the slide for a box of the slides) dissolves into the one replacing it, opacity alone, on the Movement's curve as a slide's dissolve is (Calm smoothstep, Bold its ease in and out: `dissolveCurve` in `render/transition-frame.ts`), in the half of the swap in which the box moves the same way: coming onto the main picture as the box comes in again (the second half), going from it as the box goes out (the first) (`pipMainDissolve` and `pipMainOpacity` in `render/pip.ts`, painted by `usePipMainDissolve` in `PipLayerView.tsx`). Through that half both are drawn, the upper added light for light over the other (`plus-lighter`; `pipMainPlaces` marks the two, `MainSlot` in `Scene.tsx` draws them), so they add up to the whole picture at every moment: never a cut, never black, and nothing on the main picture shrinks, grows or slides, whatever the box's own move (a fade, a scale or a slide, as before). The black rectangle under a moving main picture is gone, so nothing in the swap depends on the screen: the hall's screens, the stream and a key and fill pair draw it alike. Session 51 gave that picture the box's own move (Scale grew or shrank it, Slide moved it towards the box's corner) over a black rectangle (none on a key and fill pair); before Session 51 the main picture cut from one to the other at the swap's middle. Under LIVE a strip names what is in the box with **Swap**, **Take off** and its settings, and the box itself can be dragged on the LIVE picture (let go near a corner, it goes into it; the dialog's corners do the same from the keyboard). Black-out and the logo cover it; Take off plays its going out (`pipOff`) and Clear all takes it off at once; Put it back brings it back; a Look can leave it out of a group (the **Picture in picture** layer in Screens' Look settings; a Look saved before shows it: `leftOut` in `src/shared/looks.ts`), and since Session 51 a key and fill group leaves it out unless its Look turns it on, in Looks saved before and since alike (kept as `turnedOn`; a group's defaults follow its role as it is now, `src/main/db/looks.ts`). The stream's Slides layout and a stage screen's audience picture box show it, and since Session 51 the Camera layout's full-frame moments too (a picture or video going live, or a picture slide): those are drawn by the hall's own Scene, with only the background, the slide and the box (`program/full-frame.ts`), so the box comes, swaps and goes there as on the hall's screens, while the props, the Lower Thirds panel's lower third and the messages stay the Camera layout's own, over it; the stream's own camera never has the box over it. Nodes draw a box of the slides or of media; a video input or a window in it is drawn on this computer's screens only. No sound comes from it (a video in it plays muted and loops). Its place and look are remembered in the library's settings (`setting.pip.place`, `src/main/pip/pip-places.ts`), as the operator saves them and as the box is moved. Restart recovery puts it back, and names a window that was in it without putting that back. Since Session 51 a video input in it is checked as the background's is: one gone from this computer's list, or whose camera the screens do not find (they have up to 20 s to say), leaves the box off, and the notice says "The video input “Input 1” in the picture-in-picture box was not there after the restart, so the box was not put back." (`pipInputGone`; `tests/e2e/pip-input-recovery.spec.ts`); and Put it back (or Back) brings a box's input back as Media has it now, or not at all once it is deleted. A phone names it ("Picture in picture: …") with **Take off** (`POST /api/v1/clear/pip`) and can never set one up, swap or move it; Simple Mode can only take it off. `tests/e2e/pip.spec.ts`.

The first time Drashti starts, its welcome offers **Set Up Screens…** (afterwards: **View > Set Up Screens…**, on Windows press Alt for the menu bar, or **Set Up Screens…** in Screens; never in Simple Mode), which opens on its displays (Session 44). **Show each display's number on it** puts a big number across every display for five seconds (not the one the controls are on), so volunteers can see which output feeds which screens. For each display it asks what it shows, one click among **The audience picture**, **The stage view (performers)** and **Not used** (the first display free for the hall already chosen when no screen shows the hall's picture), and a kirtan's languages on it; **Done** sets the screens there and then (Session 44). **More setup…** goes on to the sound output, with **Play a test tone** (a short beep through the audio player, on the output being tried, before anything is chosen), the stream and the default theme, and nothing changes until **Finish**. Done and Finish set it all at once: outputs join a group with their role and languages (a group whose screens all change the same way just changes, keeping its name; otherwise one named "Audience" or "Stage" is made), a display already in use keeps its screen and canvas, and **Not used** takes a display's output away; displays that are not connected are left alone; an output on the controls' display asks first, as in Screens. Then every screen shows a test slide for eight seconds: its name, what it shows and its languages, and a sample kirtan slide in those languages (nothing live changes). Since Session 50 the wizard marks **The controls are on this display** wherever it offers that display (in Finish's summary too), and Done and Finish wait for **Keep these screens?** (see "Keeping the controls reachable"): gone back, the wizard is at its displays again, with the choices as made. A screen that Audience or Stage switched off counts as in use there, as the wizard opens and as Keep these screens? goes (Session 51). **Skip this step** keeps that step's setting as it is, and closing the wizard keeps everything. **Show on** (Session 44, `src/renderer/src/screens/ShowOn.tsx`) is quicker still: while no screen shows the hall's picture and a display other than the controls' is connected, an info notice under the header (never over the show controls) offers **Show on** and the display's name, and the first start's welcome makes it its primary; one click makes it the hall's screen with the setup's own defaults (an "Audience" group in the live Look, every language, the screen named after the display) and puts the test slide up for a few seconds, while Keep these screens? asks (Session 50). **Not now** puts the offer away until the displays change. Before, from a new library with a second display, the hall's picture took 9 clicks from the welcome and 11 from the menu; now 1 from the offer or the welcome, 3 from the menu (counted by hand; `tests/e2e/show-on.spec.ts` checks the one click). Drashti never changes a display's resolution or refresh rate.

1. Open **Screens**, add a group (for example "Main Hall"), and press **Use this display** next to each display that feeds the audience. Set the canvas size and scaling if a screen needs something other than 1920 × 1080 fit. **Identify screens** shows each screen's name on it. The setup is saved and comes back on the next start. If a display is missing at startup, its screen says "Display not connected" and opens by itself when the display returns.
2. Pick a presentation in the library (**Library**, at the top of the left column) or an item in a playlist, then click a slide, or press Space or the right arrow, to put it on the screens. The slides fill the middle in play order, flowing on from one group to the next; each has its group's colour beside its number, and the first slide each time a group comes up carries the group's name. The slider above them sets their size (Drashti remembers it).
3. Use the clear buttons along the bottom (or F1 to F8) and **Black-out** (B) at their right. A layer's clear is lit, with a dot, while that layer has something on the screens.

**Looks.** A Look says what every screen group shows: which layers its screens draw (background, slides, props, the picture-in-picture box since Session 50, messages, the ticker and the Masks layer; a Look saved before the box shows it, but in a key and fill group, which since Session 51 leaves it out until a Look turns it on), a kirtan's languages and their order, and whether slides are drawn as designed or as a lower third (the slide's words in a box along the bottom, as the stream's Camera layout draws them; a slide with no words but a picture is drawn as designed). Over a lower third, messages go along the top, as in the stream's Camera layout, and the words sit above the ticker, so nothing covers them. A lower third holds at most 6 lines (`LOWER_THIRD_MAX_LINES` in `src/shared/program.ts`, Shastra's passages counted by the same); when the live slide has more where one is drawn now (a group's Look, or the stream's Camera and words), a note under LIVE says so: "Lower third shows 6 of 7 lines" (Sessions 31–36, SLD-8; `tests/e2e/lower-thirds.spec.ts`). One Look is live at a time. **Looks** in the right column switches it: every group changes in one change of the show, in step. In Screens, under **Looks**: **New Look**, **Duplicate**, rename, **Up**/**Down** and **Remove** (never the last one; removing the live one puts the first live); the group cards below show and change each group's settings in the Look chosen there. Drashti starts with the first Look after a clean quit, so a volunteer in Simple Mode never starts on last week's festival Look; after an unexpected stop the Look that was live comes back, and the recovery notice says so. Simple Mode keeps the live Look: the engine refuses switching it from anywhere, and the Looks cannot be changed. The upgrade to Looks (migration 20) made **Standard** from the groups as they were, so every screen shows exactly what it did. The stream group takes its languages and its Movement from a Look: its layout is the stream's own setting (YouTube's card in Screens and the Stream panel show the same one), and the Program keeps its own rules. **Movement** (Session 27): each group's screens move **Calm** (the default: slides fade in place) or **Bold** (the words may move, wipe and zoom; pictures and backgrounds only ever dissolve), chosen per group in each Look (an admin, Pro Mode; never in Simple Mode; the stage group always cuts). A transition's length is the slide's whatever the Movement, and a kind a Calm group cannot draw is drawn as Calm's dissolve there (`docs/design.md` section 40). The setup wizard's languages go into the live Look, and a group the wizard makes gets them in every Look. Phones (Remote devices) switch Looks in the remote's **More** tab, and scripts with `POST /api/v1/looks/{id}/live` (`docs/api.md`).

**Stage layouts.** A stage group shows the **Standard** stage screen (the slide and the next one, notes, the clock, running timers and the stage message, making room as they come and go), or a layout of boxes made in Drashti. In Screens, a stage group's settings in a Look have **Stage layout** and **Edit stage layouts…**: the editor lists Standard (built in, shown as it is) and the layouts made; **Duplicate** turns Standard (or any layout) into boxes to change. Each box is one of: the current slide, the next slide, notes, the clock, one timer or every running timer, the stage message, the playlist's next items (headers too), the time left on the video or sound playing, the audience screens' state (black-out, logo), fixed text, or (Session 50) **The audience picture, live**. That box draws exactly what this computer's first audience group shows at that moment, from the same state by the same Scene as the hall's own output (`render/AudienceBox.tsx`, `render/audience-picture.ts`): the background (a video, a video input, a window), the slide, props, the lower third, messages, the ticker and the picture-in-picture box, black-out and the logo too, the hall's canvas in a 16:9 picture with black around it. It is added under the boxes already there, so the stage message, the clock and the timers draw over it; it has a place, a size and a label only, and no built-in layout has it. A stage screen showing it loads what comes next, as the hall's screens do (`tests/e2e/stage-layouts.spec.ts`); on a node it draws that node's own audience picture, or, on a node with no audience screen of its own that is on (one turned off in **Screens** shows nothing), Main's hall as the live Look sets it (Session 51: Main sends its hall with each of a node's stage screens, `hall` in `NodeScreen`; before, such a node drew every layer regardless; `tests/e2e/stage-hall-picture.spec.ts:598`). The stage display in a browser draws it too since Session 51 (`GET /api/v1/stage` says the hall), as the remote's live picture is drawn: pictures and videos as the same small previews, under the same rules (a Stage device may fetch them as a Remote does), and black for a video input or a window, which are on Drashti's computer, named there in a small muted line in the box's picture's bottom left corner (its top left until Session 52) in the phone remote's words ("Video input: Input 1", "A window: …", "Picture in picture: …"), as on a node's stage screen; before, it said "The audience picture shows on Drashti's own stage screens" instead. With this computer's Audience off the box keeps the picture, the one the hall comes back to, and says "Audience off" on the warning's amber (Session 51; `off` in `hallPicture`'s answer, only when this computer's switch closed the hall's screens, never for a hall shown on a node alone) in its picture's bottom left corner, 40 px in each way, with any line naming what that page cannot draw under it (Session 52, `WORDS_CORNER` in `render/audience-picture.ts`: in the top left, a stage message along the top covered it), on the stage screens, a node's, a tablet's, the operator's stage preview and the layout editor alike, so the performers are not misled; the box of the audience screens' state then says "Audience screens off", and the Standard stage screen "NOW · AUDIENCE SCREENS OFF", before black-out or the logo. With no audience group at all (Session 52) there is no hall's picture to draw: the box is black, with no Scene, and says "No audience screens" (`NO_AUDIENCE`) in one line in its bottom left corner, in the stage's muted grey and regular weight, 64 px on the whole stage and never below 30 px while it fits (`noAudienceLine`), wrapping and shrinking only as far as it must, down to 8 px in a very small box (`fittedSize`; `data-audience="none"`); before, it drew every layer and language at 1920 × 1080, a picture no hall's screen shows. Until a page knows the hall it is black alone, with no words (`data-audience="unknown"`): a tablet before Drashti's first answer, the operator window before it has the screens (`useHallPicture` is undefined then). `tests/e2e/stage-hall-picture.spec.ts` checks a video input, a window and a box under the stage box, Audience off clear of a stage message, no audience group, a tablet and a node (each with no audience group too), and a tablet naming a window it cannot draw. Drag a box to move it (it snaps to the canvas's edges and middle and to the other boxes) and its handles to resize it, or type its place and size; each box has its text size (or "as large as fit"), colour, alignment and a label, and the layout a background colour. **Save** puts it on any stage screen whose live Look uses it. Layouts are drawn on 1920 × 1080 and scaled to each stage screen's canvas. The stage display in a browser draws its group's layout the same way. The time left on a video or sound is known once a screen or the audio player has loaded the file (Drashti keeps it in the library from then on).

**Masks.** A mask is shapes (rectangles, rounded rectangles and ellipses) on a canvas of its own size that hide what is under them, or the inverse, show only what is inside them; it is stretched over each screen it goes on, and what it hides becomes see-through (the screen's black, or nothing on a key output). Masks apply in two places, on purpose. **A group's own mask in a Look** is its screens' shape (an LED wall's outline, a projector spilling onto a pillar): in Screens, a group's settings in a Look have **Mask** and **Edit masks…**; it is over everything on those screens, the logo included, and Clear all, F8 and Simple Mode never take it away, because the shape of a screen is not something to clear by mistake mid-sabha. **The Masks layer** is for a moment in the show (a circle round a video, a band for the words): the **Masks** panel in the right column puts a mask from the library up on the audience screens (a group's Look can leave the layer out), and F8, Clear all and the panel take it down; Put it back and recovery bring it back as any layer. Stage screens ignore masks; the stream keeps its own rules (the Masks layer in the Slides layout, nothing in Camera and words). The mask editor (from the panel's **Edit**, or Screens) lists the masks, draws the chosen one over the hall screen's live picture (Session 44: the first audience group's first screen as it is now, without its Look's masks, for as long as the editor is open, for an admin or an operator; what the mask hides darkened; a test pattern, saying why, when no hall screen is set up), and places shapes by dragging (with snapping) or by typing; **Save** puts the change on any screen showing it. On the screens a mask is drawn as its cover, black where it hides, laid over the picture (Session 15): the same pixels as masking the layers, but a video under it is not drawn again through the mask on every frame.

**Key and fill.** A screen group set to **Key and fill (for a video switcher)** in Screens drives two outputs, each on its own display, into a video switcher such as an ATEM: the **fill** is the group's picture, black where it is empty, and the **key** is white wherever the fill has something (grey where it is half see-through) and black elsewhere, so the switcher can lay the graphics over a camera. The first display used is the fill and the next the key (**Sends** on each screen swaps them; since Session 51 Screens' **Shows** and **Sends** ask Keep these screens?). What it shows comes from its Look; a group that becomes key and fill starts, in every Look, with the slide's words as a lower third, props and messages, and no backgrounds, and since Session 51 it leaves the picture-in-picture box out unless its Look turns it on. The fill is drawn over black, so set the switcher's key to pre-multiplied. Black-out and the logo are for the hall: they take these graphics off. Both outputs follow the same engine state and clock, so a dissolve starts at the same moment on both (the end-to-end test also measures how many frames apart the two windows paint each change), and since Session 51 a lower third and the picture-in-picture box are worked out from the engine's time at every drawing, so once both windows draw the show and have seen the same changes, the key and the fill draw them alike at every moment, the frame in which a move ends included (`tests/e2e/lower-thirds.spec.ts` holds moments before and after each end; the end frame itself has no end-to-end test). Each window starts drawing the show at its own moment (after its own warm-up, up to 8 s; again when Audience is turned on; after a reload), and a slide's lower third starts still in a window that starts drawing, as a screen that joins part-way always has: a move running then is drawn moving on one and at rest on the other until it ends (at most 780 ms). DeckLink cards (hardware key and fill over SDI) wait for the audit.

**Shastra.** Texts such as a Vachanamrut or Satsang Diksha come from files an admin prepares from a source BAPS or the mandir has authorised (Drashti ships none; the format is in `docs/shastra-format.md`, with two made-up examples in `docs/examples/`). Drag one onto the library, or use **Shastra › Texts… › Load a text…**; loading a text again updates it (same abbreviation), and its passages in playlists keep working. In the library's **Shastra** tab, type a reference in the box (`PG 14`, `PG 14-16`, `PV P.Pr. 1`; case, dots and spaces don't matter) and press Enter, search the words in any language (accents ignored), or browse a text by its sections; the passage shows in the slide grid like a presentation, ready to go up, and dragged onto a playlist it becomes an item (a template's slot can ask for one: **Add a slot…**, _A Shastra passage_). A reference that names nothing says why. Each item starts a slide with its reference line; a long one goes on over several slides, cut at line breaks or sentences, every language alike. The slides are drawn with the text's theme (Themes has a style for each language, Sanskrit's two scripts included, and for the reference line, above or below the words) and fitted to where the live Look shows them: each group's languages, and lower thirds (the stream's too, on air) take fewer lines. **Sanskrit is two languages**, Devanagari and Gujarati script, so each group's Look chooses the script its people read; a text given in one script is written in the other by Drashti, and transliteration a file leaves out is made from the Sanskrit (every "a" said), both marked as made. Phones put up a reference from the remote's **More** tab, and scripts with `POST /api/v1/shastra`. Loading, a text's theme and removing texts are Pro Mode only.

**MIDI.** A MIDI controller (pads, a keyboard, a foot switch) plugged into the computer: **MIDI controller…** in More tools (Session 44; the Macros panel's before) chooses the device and maps its notes and controllers. **Learn**, then press a pad: it now does Next, Back, Clear all, Black-out or Logo. A note acts as it goes down; a controller as it rises past its middle. The mappings are kept in the library's settings, and the device is found again by its name whenever it is plugged in. Only the operator window may use MIDI (the permission is given to that page alone, and SysEx never). In Simple Mode the pads still work. A pad mapped to a macro before Session 44 stays in the settings and does nothing.

**Simple Mode.** **Simple Mode** in the header (or **View > Switch to Simple Mode**) turns the window into one screen with big buttons for a volunteer: the playlist on the left (pick it at the top), what is on the screens and what comes next, and **Back**, **Next**, **Black-out**, **Logo** and **Clear all** along the bottom, each showing its key since Session 51 (under its words; Black-out's B and the Logo's L only while the single-letter keys are on). The keys keep working, including a presentation clicker's (Page Down and Up, B or .), and Next starts the playlist when nothing is live. **Back** undoes the last Next exactly (one Next too many into a video takes the video down again), as long as nothing else changed in between; otherwise it is Previous. **Black-out** and **Logo** cover the picture without clearing it, so pressing them again brings back exactly what was there, and the stage screens keep working. Straight after **Clear all**, **Put it back** (or Cmd/Ctrl+Z; since Session 51 never with Shift as well: Shift+Cmd+Z is Edit › Redo's key on a Mac) brings everything back, with videos and sound carrying on. Nothing in Simple Mode can change the library, playlists, props, messages, timers, themes, the screens or the sound, and Back Up and Restore leave the File menu: the window does not offer them, and the main process refuses them too (`src/main/simple-mode.ts`). Since Session 50 it may clear a video input and take a picture-in-picture box off, never put either up from Media (`SIMPLE_MODE_REFUSED_COMMANDS` in `src/shared/mode.ts`; since Session 51 a playlist's video input item goes up through its Next or a click on the item, as any item does), and may turn Audience and Stage on, never off. Since Session 20 a start after a quit on purpose is in Pro Mode (with roles on too since Session 46, next), and after an unexpected stop Drashti comes back in the mode it was in (`startingMode` in `src/shared/mode.ts`). Since Session 21 that takes the same answer as restart recovery, `recentStop` from `startupRecovery`: only a stop less than 3 hours before the start (`RECOVERY_MAX_AGE_MS`) brings Simple Mode back, whether or not anything was on the screens; a stop longer ago starts as after a quit on purpose. Leaving takes a deliberate step: **Switch to Pro Mode…** at the top right of Simple Mode (or **View > Switch to Pro Mode…**; on Windows press Alt for the menu bar), then type **pro**, or with roles on a PIN (next). **Logo** shows the prop marked as the logo in Pro Mode (Props, the stamp button), on black, instead of the picture; **F12** (since Session 50) and **L** do the same in both modes, and Pro Mode has **Logo** and, after Clear all, **Put it back** beside the layer clears. **Its first steps** (Sessions 31–36, `simple/SimpleFirstRun.tsx`): the first time Simple Mode opens on a library with no slide up, three short steps show under the header (choosing tonight's playlist; Next and Back; Black-out, Clear all and Put it back), in the window's flow so they never cover the big buttons, and taking no keys. **Next step** goes on; **Skip**, **Done** or a slide going up ends them, and they never come again for that library. "Seen" is kept in the library's settings (`simpleFirstRun`) through `app:simple-first-run-seen`, which Simple Mode allows (the Pro welcome's `setup:welcome-seen` stays locked); tests start without them unless they set `DRASHTI_TEST_NO_WIZARD=0` (`tests/e2e/simple-first-run.spec.ts`).

**Waiting** (Sessions 31–36). `Loading` says it waits only after half a second (`LOADING_AFTER_MS` in `ui/States.tsx`), so a quick answer never flashes a word; where a wait is known to be long (starting the stream's picture, asking for an update) it is said at once (`immediate`). Its `role="status"` box is there from the start, the words hidden until then, so a screen reader hears them as they come (`tests/e2e/guides.spec.ts` times it in the page with a slow guide: hidden at 100 ms, shown by 600 ms). The phone remote's waits (the playlists, the library, the messages, a search) are `role="status"` lines, and its empty places say where they are filled: "There are no playlists yet: they are made in Drashti on the computer, in Pro Mode." (`tests/e2e/remote.spec.ts` holds the phone's requests to see each wait).

**Roles and PINs** (Session 14, `src/shared/roles.ts`). A volunteer runs a sabha in Simple Mode; an **operator** runs the show in Pro Mode (playlists, words and slides, props, messages, timers and lower thirds, switching Looks, putting up masks, video inputs and picture in picture, turning Audience and Stage off and on, announcements, going live, ending and recording); an **admin** also sets Drashti up: importing, removing and converting, naming this computer's video inputs (Session 50), themes and the logo, Shastra texts, calendars, the idle rotation and every schedule (backups), MIDI, the screens, Looks, stage layouts, masks and the sound output, the setup wizard, the stream's settings and keys, phones and nodes, backups, restores and updates, and the PINs. Roles are **off** until an admin sets two PINs in **File > Roles and PINs…** (or the setup wizard's **PINs** step): an admin PIN and an operator PIN, 4 to 12 digits, different from each other. Off, nothing changes from before. On:

- Drashti starts in Pro Mode after a clean quit, as the operator, admin locked, with no PIN asked (Session 46, Priyansh's choice; until then it started in Simple Mode, so that a volunteer who switched the computer on met Simple Mode); after an unexpected stop less than 3 hours before the start it comes back in the mode it was in, whether or not anything was on the screens, so the operator, or a volunteer in Simple Mode, carries on (admin locked); after a stop longer ago, in Pro Mode as after a clean quit. Whoever starts Drashti can run and edit the show: the PINs guard setting up and leaving Simple Mode. With roles on and the admin PIN not typed, neither the first start's welcome nor the setup wizard opens by itself (`setup:state`).
- Leaving Simple Mode takes either PIN in place of the word: the operator PIN gives Pro Mode as an operator, the admin PIN unlocks admin as well.
- In Pro Mode, an admin control asks for the admin PIN before it acts (the window's bridge asks the main process whether admin is locked before every admin request, and waits for the PIN); a menu item asks the same way. Admin then stays unlocked until **Lock** or quitting (Session 44; until then it locked itself 10 minutes after the last admin action, and the header counted down): the header shows **Operator**, or **Admin · Lock** where the countdown was (press it to lock at once); going into Simple Mode locks it too, and a new start begins locked, as before.
- The main process checks every admin request itself (`ADMIN_CHANNELS`, refused by `src/main/ipc/handle.ts` beside Simple Mode's refusals), so nothing the window or a page does can get round it. Phones and nodes keep their kinds: a Remote device runs the show (an operator's requests), and pairing devices and nodes is an admin's.
- PINs are kept only as scrypt hashes (each with its own salt, about a tenth of a second to check, run off the main thread so the show never waits) in `roles.json` in the data folder: never in the library, so never in a backup, and a restore never turns roles off. After **five wrong PINs** in a row nothing is checked for a minute, doubling with each wrong PIN after that up to 15 minutes; the count is kept in the same file, so a restart does not reset it. The log says when a PIN was wrong, never the PIN.

**Importing.** Drag files or folders onto the library's presentation list (its sheet, from **Library**), or use **Import…** above it (files, or a whole folder), or drop them on a playlist. Progress shows in the status bar along the bottom, with **Cancel**. When the import ends, the report opens, unless a slide is live (then **Report** in the status bar opens it). The report shows what came across and lists each problem with its fix: **Replace**, **Keep both** or **Skip** for a file that changed since it was imported, **Try again**, **Find…** for missing media, and **Open** to check an imported presentation. Earlier reports stay available from the report's list of earlier imports.

**Arrangements.** A presentation plays in the order its arrangement sets: its groups as the arrangement lists them, repeats included, so a chorus sung three times shows three times in the slide grid and comes up each time with Next. Choose another arrangement, or **All slides in order**, above the slides; the choice stays with the presentation. Changing it while the presentation is live keeps the slide on screen, and Next carries on in the new order. Imported presentations keep the arrangement they were set to play in, and lyrics files with a repeated header play as written.

**Sabha templates.** **Templates**, beside **Playlists** at the top of the left column, lists running orders to make playlists from, kept apart from the week's playlists so a template is never run by mistake: clicking a template's items never shows them on the screens, and the engine refuses to play one. Two examples come with Drashti, **Example: Ravi Sabha** and **Example: Bal/Kishore Sabha**, made of headers and slots; the mandir's real running orders replace them at the setup. **Use** (or **New playlist from this** in a template, or its menu) makes a playlist from a template, in the selected folder, named with the day for you to change. A template keeps headers, fixed items (the welcome slide, the arti) and slots: named places such as "Kirtan" or "Pravachan title", each with the category its search starts at. **Save as template…** in a playlist's menu makes one from any playlist: each presentation stays the same every time or becomes a slot (a kirtan becomes a slot named after its category, unless you choose otherwise); placeholders become slots. **Add a slot…** adds one to a template or a playlist. Clicking a slot (or a placeholder from an import) opens **Fill**: a list starting with the kirtans of the slot's category, a category to change, and a search box (titles, words, kavi and raag; Enter takes the first); choosing one puts it in the slot's place. A slot that asks for a Shastra passage takes a reference or the passage's words instead. **Edit slot…** in a slot's menu (right-click it) renames it and changes its category. In the show, an empty slot is stepped over like a header.

**Timers in the running order.** **Timers when it goes up…** in an item's menu (right-click it) makes the item start a timer from the beginning, reset one, or show one on the audience screens (as a message with its name and time), as the item goes up: the pravachan item, say, starts a 30-minute countdown, which the stage screens show while it runs. They happen in the same change as the item, when Next, a click or a phone takes the show to it, not when Back or Previous return to it; the item says what it does under its name. Templates keep them, and a playlist made from a template gets them.

**The idle rotation.** For the time before a sabha, or a lobby screen: darshan pictures and quotes, each up for a set time, dissolving into the next. Drashti ships neither: **Set up** in the **Idle rotation** panel (at the bottom of the live column, Pro Mode) ticks pictures from the media library (put in order with the arrows), sets how long each is up (3 to 600 seconds), and keeps the admin's **quotes** (words in Gujarati, Hindi and English, any of them, and who said or wrote them), typed from authorised sources; **Then the quote of the day** adds one of them after the pictures: one quote per date, the same all day, the next one the next day, which a stage layout's **Quote of the day** box shows too. Which screens show the rotation is set in each group's Look, under **When nothing is up**: **The idle rotation, once started** shows it after the operator presses **Start**, while nothing is up on those screens; it **stops by itself** when the first slide or picture goes up (whatever puts it up), so a later Clear all leaves the screens empty rather than bringing it back; **The idle rotation, always** (a lobby screen) shows it whenever nothing is up there, started or not; **Nothing** is the default. Every screen works out the item and its dissolve from the engine's clock, so all screens change together; quotes show in the group's languages. A picture removed from the library leaves the rotation. After an unexpected stop it is not running (lobby screens show it anyway; press **Start** again for the hall). Simple Mode can neither change the pictures nor the quotes.

**Samvat and tithi.** Drashti computes no tithi and ships no calendar: the admin loads a calendar file prepared from a source BAPS or the mandir has authorised (the format is in `docs/calendar-format.md`, with a made-up example in `docs/examples/`, and `node scripts/placeholder-calendar.mjs 60 > placeholder-calendar.json` writes a made-up one from today to try). Drag it onto the library, or use **Calendar** in the Timers panel, then **Load a calendar…**; loading a calendar again (same name) updates it, and where two calendars give a date the one loaded last is used. Today's entry, for this computer's date, goes into the show's state and changes just after midnight, so every window shows the same: **the operator window** along the bottom ("Samvat 2082, … · festival", in English; a click opens Calendar), Simple Mode too; **stage screens** through a stage layout's **Samvat date and tithi** box or a **Clock** box with **Today's Samvat date under the time**, in Gujarati (with Gujarati digits) or English; and **the audience screens** only through a message whose field is set to _Today's Samvat date_, so it is up only when the operator shows it. A date no calendar gives shows nothing anywhere. **Calendar** lists the calendars with their dates and removes one; loading and removing are Pro Mode only.

**Playlists.** The left column lists playlists and folders (Session 26a); since Session 44 they fill it, and the library opens over them as a sheet from the **Library** button at its top (`src/renderer/src/operator/LibrarySheet.tsx`: not modal, so the show's keys still work; Esc or Close shuts it; it stands aside while one of its rows is dragged, so a playlist's name under it takes the drop). Click a playlist to open it in the middle of the window, above the slides (**×** closes it). A playlist made from a folder (a smart playlist, one marked so in its settings as a Dropbox folder link's is, or an earlier one whose files lie in the folders its headers name; `PlaylistRepo.fromFolderIds`) is shown a folder at a time (Session 44, `src/shared/playlist-folders.ts`, read from its headers, so nothing new is stored): the folder's own items in the middle under crumbs back up ("Placeholder Sabha › Week 1"), its folders at the bottom of the left column (`PlaylistFolders.tsx`, with a splitter above), and a click on the playlist goes back to the top folder. Next, Simple Mode, the phones and the API follow the whole playlist, and the middle follows the live item's folder; with nothing chosen, an add goes at the end of the folder shown. Its items are cards (`src/renderer/src/playlists/OpenPlaylist.tsx`: a picture from `IPC.playlists.firstSlides` or the media's still, the name, what it is and its length or slide count), with **Add files…** and **From a Link…** in its header. **+ New** beside **Playlists** makes a playlist or a folder (inside the selected folder, or beside the selected playlist) and lets you name it straight away; the chosen row shows **Rename** and **Delete**. Drag presentations, or media from the library's **Media** tab (and since Session 51 its video inputs), into an open playlist where you want them, or onto a playlist's name to add them at the end. Without dragging (Session 25): choose a presentation, media file, Shastra passage or (Session 51) video input in the library and press **Add to playlist** under it, or choose **Add to “…”** in its row's menu (right-click, Shift+F10 or the Menu key); it goes after the playlist's chosen item, or at the end when none is chosen. Drag items to reorder them (Cmd/Ctrl-click and Shift-click mark several, as do Cmd/Ctrl-A and a box dragged from an empty place: Session 44's choosing many, `src/renderer/src/ui/select-many.tsx`), or choose one and press its **Up** or **Down** (on a line under it), or **Alt+↑**/**Alt+↓** on it: the item keeps the keyboard and a screen reader hears "Moved “…” up". Each add and move is one Undo step. **⋯ > Add a header** adds a header; double-click a header or the playlist's name to rename it. Right-click a playlist, folder or item (or use **⋯**, or press Shift+F10 or the Menu key on it) for the rest. Items the import could not find are placeholders, drawn with a dashed amber border and counted as "missing" in the list of playlists: drop a presentation on one to put it there. **Delete** or **Backspace** removes the marked items at once; removing a playlist or a folder (with the playlists in it) asks first. Undo brings back any of these, as it does presentations. Removed playlists and items are deleted for good after 30 days. Dragging (Session 26a) never changes a row's size as it starts (Chromium cancels a drag whose row changes under the pointer, `src/renderer/src/playlists/drag.ts`): Add to playlist, Up and Down hide in place, and a row is chosen once the drag ends. Search results drag as rows do, and files dropped from the Finder or Explorer on the open playlist (or a playlist's name) are imported and added where they land (`importIntoPlaylist`, `src/shared/import-items.ts`).

**Deleting** (Session 26a). Every row that can be deleted has a trash button (`src/renderer/src/ui/RowDelete.tsx`), shown on the chosen row (a Tab stop, last in the row's Tab order) and the row under the mouse, keeping its room so a row never changes size: a playlist or folder, a playlist item ("Remove … from the playlist", out of it only), a presentation, media, music and its tracks. It runs what the Delete key does, with the same question and Undo, and **Delete…** is in each row's menu. **Choosing many** (Session 44, `src/renderer/src/ui/select-many.tsx`), in every list one can delete from (playlists, a playlist's items, presentations, media, audio playlists and their sounds, lower thirds): a box dragged from an empty place chooses the rows it touches (the list scrolls by itself near its top and bottom), Shift-click a run, Cmd/Ctrl-click one more, Cmd/Ctrl-A the whole list; a bar says how many, with **Delete** for them all and **Clear**, and Delete or Backspace does the same: one question where one is asked, and one Undo brings them all back. No `aria-multiselectable` (lists of buttons, not listboxes): a status line says how many are chosen, and each row says `aria-pressed`. The show controls are never in such a list. **Media**, which could not be removed before, is deleted with `src/main/library/media-removal.ts` (migration 36): the question says where it is used (presentations' slides and cues, kirtans' sounds, playlists, music, props, themes, the idle rotation, and whether it is up now); deleting marks the media row and the playlist items and music tracks holding it with one moment, so Undo puts back exactly those; while deleted it reads as a missing file everywhere (`MediaRepo`, the cue query, the protocol), except that `drashti-media://` still serves it while it is up (`mediaUpNow`, `src/shared/node-media.ts`). At the next start its file and still go and the row stays as a missing file, so what pointed at it loads and saves as with any missing file; importing the same bytes again (or a conversion making them) brings it back to life (`media-store.ts`). Music lists and tracks are marked when removed too, and gone for good at the next start. **Folders as playlists** (Session 26c). `src/shared/folder-order.ts` orders a folder's files folder by folder, depth first (`Intl.Collator` numeric, case-blind), each folder's by kind (videos, decks, other presentations, pictures, sounds), with a header named by the path ("Week 1 › Videos") before each inside folder's part. A Dropbox folder link's import ends in `makeFolderPlaylist` (`src/main/playlists/folder-playlist.ts`): a playlist at the top of the list (`PlaylistRepo.createAtTop`), named after the folder (" (2)" when taken), made from the import report (a 1080p copy stands for its original), opened by the window (`LinkView.playlist`); its PDFs and pictures wait in `LinkView.waiting` until `IPC.links.addWaiting`, which imports them and puts each at the end of its folder's part (`addToFolderParts`). The import report's Add to playlist uses the same order for a folder (`itemsMadeBy`). **Smart playlists** (Session 26c). A playlist's `kind` is `normal`, `smart` or `repeat`, with JSON `settings` (migration 38): a smart one's settings name the folder it follows. `SmartFolders` (`src/main/playlists/smart-folders.ts`) follows each: `fs.watch` (recursive) where it works plus a scan every 10 s, and a catch-up at start. A file new or changed since it was imported (`smart_files`: its size and time, and its item) is ready once its size and time have held still for 3 s; ready files go through the normal import (`onConflict: 'replace'`, so a deck is replaced in place) and join at the end of their folder's part (`addToFolderParts`), a changed media file's item pointing at the new media (`PlaylistRepo.retarget`). A file gone is `missing` (the item's `gone`), and `IPC.playlists.removeMissing` takes those items out (`removed`; Undo through `restoreItems` marks them missing again). A folder gone is `folder-gone`; more than `SMART_MAX_FILES` (2,000) is `too-many`, when nothing is added or marked. `IPC.playlists.createSmart` is an admin's, locked in Simple Mode with `removeMissing`; the window hears `IPC.playlists.smartChanged`. Test switches (never in a packaged Drashti): `DRASHTI_TEST_SMART_SCAN_MS` and `DRASHTI_TEST_SMART_SETTLE_MS`. **Repeat Slides** (Session 26d). A `repeat` playlist's settings are its seconds each and transition (`src/shared/repeat.ts`, checked by `repeatSettingsSchema`); `RepeatSlides` (`src/main/playlists/repeat-slides.ts`) plays it as the presentation `repeat:<playlistId>`, its steps each picture and each slide of each presentation, with the playlist's time and transition and no cues, looping, so the engine's auto-advance times every step on every screen. The engine's `repeat` (`RepeatRun`) says it is going round: `startRepeat` (and any of its slides put up) sets it, `stopRepeat` (Esc, Stop, LIVE's Stop, `POST /api/v1/repeat/stop`) clears it and the slide stays; anything else on the slide layer clears it in `applyAt`; Put it back sets it again; recovery saves it. A count running when the settings change keeps going (they take effect from the next slide). The transitions (`TransitionKind`: `cut`, `dissolve`, `fade-black`, `slide`, `zoom`) are drawn by `transitionFrame` in the slide layer, timed from `shownAt`; a slow zoom's span is its slide's time up (`spanMs`, set by the engine). Test switch (never in a packaged Drashti): `DRASHTI_TEST_CLOCK_HOLD=1` gives the main process `drashtiTestHoldClock(at)`, which holds every output's clock at one moment. **Save as MP4** (Session 26e). `src/main/convert/trim.ts` builds the copy: FFmpeg finds the keyframe at or before the start by listing keyframes from there without accurate seeking (`-noaccurate_seek -skip_frame nokey`, showinfo, stopped at the first: where a copy from that seek begins), then copies every picture and sound stream from it (`-ss <keyframe> -i … -c copy -map 0:V? -map 0:a? -map_metadata 0 -movflags +faststart`); `mp4Problem` refuses streams an MP4 cannot hold. `TrimSaves` (`trim-saves.ts`) asks where (a save dialog, never over a file there) and runs it through `ConvertService.saveCopy`, the same queue, priority, on-air wait, 2 GB check and hidden part-file as the 1080p copies, stopped by `cancelFile`; the window hears `IPC.media.savesChanged`. The timeline (`src/renderer/src/markers/Timeline.tsx`) draws its stills from a hidden copy of the video. Test switch (never in a packaged Drashti): `DRASHTI_TEST_SAVE_READRATE=1` makes FFmpeg read no faster than the video plays, so a test can quit during a save. **Pictures' looks** (Session 26e). `PictureLook` (`src/shared/picture-look.ts`: zoom, move, brightness, contrast, colour, sharpness) is kept as JSON in `media.look` (migration 39); `autoLevels` sets brightness and contrast from a luminance histogram (1 % and 99 %). The engine's state carries the looks of the pictures up and coming next (`pictures`, from `mediaInState`) and the live zoom (`liveZoom`, `zoomLive`/`resetLiveZoom`, ending with its showing: a slide's `shownAt` or a background's `startedAt`; Simple Mode refuses both), so every screen, node, phone and the stream draw them from the same state; the window's thumbnails read `IPC.media.looks`. `Looked` (`src/renderer/src/render/picture-looks.tsx`) draws a picture with its look: a transform for the zoom and move, CSS `brightness`/`contrast`/`saturate`, and its sharpened copy; a picture without a look is drawn exactly as before. **Sharpened copies** (Session 26f). Sharpening in steps of 5 % was an SVG `feConvolveMatrix` filter on every screen, half a second to a second a change without a graphics chip; now `SharpenedCopies` (`src/main/media/sharpened.ts`) makes a copy of each picture whose Sharpness is above 0, once, at its own size, with the same kernel (`sharpenPixels`, `src/shared/picture-look.ts`): in a hidden window of its own (`src/renderer/sharpen.html`, `createSharpenWindow`) at the lowest priority, which decodes the picture as the screens do, sharpens its pixels and hands back a JPEG (from a JPEG, quality 0.95) or a PNG, through `IPC.sharpen.next`/`made`; one at a time, the window closed when none is left. Copies are `Media/sharpened/<sha256>-<step>.jpg|png`, read again from the library (`MediaRepo.sharpenWants`) at start, when a look is kept and when the library changes: one of another step or of a picture gone is removed, a missing one made; the purge removes a deleted picture's (`removeCopiesOf`); never made from or into anything but the picture's file. A look given to the screens and thumbnails carries `copy`, the step of its ready copy (`copyStep`), and they draw `drashti-media://sharp/<id>/<step>` (`sharpenedUrl`) once it has loaded in that page (`useSharpCopy`, held while drawn or up next, the outputs' `Preloader` loading it ahead), the picture unsharpened until then; only the Picture editor's own preview still draws the SVG filter (`lookFilter`). A node gets a ready copy as one more file of its wanted list (`copyMediaId`, `<id>_s<step>`, served by `WantedMedia.source`), fetched with the picture; a phone's preview is made from the copy (the preview route takes the copy's id), and a phone draws a background picture with its look too, as it draws a slide's (it drew a background's without one before). A live zoom glides there over 250 ms by the engine's clock. Keep this zoom is `IPC.media.keepLiveZoom`. The Performance workflow's `enhanced-pictures` scenario changes slides of 1080p pictures with every enhancement through the import, and checks every screen drew its sharpened copy and no SVG filter. With the SVG filter it missed its budgets on CI's machines, which have no graphics chip (8896f12, runs 38558022313 and 38558045275: during the import, p90 up to 761 ms on the Mac and medians of 72 and 91 ms on Windows); with the sharpened copies it passes them (d202e36, runs 38595855177, 38595881570 and 38595908004, three on each: medians 9 to 17 ms, p90 15 to 33 ms; one Mac job passed on its re-run, its first try having lost one change, as the plain check did once on the same machines). Since Session 26f a scenario at the plain check's pace warms up as the plain check does (25 changes before the import), as CI's Macs are slow for the first few seconds whatever the slides.

**LIVE, Edit layout and Add to playlist** (Session 26a). The live preview is headed **LIVE** and the operator window's Next preview is gone (`NextPreview` stays in Simple Mode; the stage screen's Next and the remote's are unchanged). **Edit layout…** on the stage panel opens the stage layout editor at the live Look's first stage group's layout (`openStageLayouts` now waits for the list when it has not been read yet). After any import the report offers **Add to playlist** (`AddImported` in `ImportReport.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/playlists/add-made.tsx`): what the import made (`itemsMadeBy`, in the order of its paths) goes at the end of the open playlist, another, or a new one named after the first path (" (2)" when taken), with Undo. Import from a Link's **When it's imported, add it to:** is chosen before Download (the open playlist when one is open) and done by `link-store.ts` when that download's run ends, dialog open or not.

**The look** (Session 26g; Sessions 39–40, `docs/design.md`). The window, Simple Mode and the component gallery take the **appearance** chosen on this computer (View › Appearance…: Follow the computer, the default, Black, White, Dhaja, Tiranga, Saffron, Peacock, Midnight Blue, Sandalwood, Lotus, Marble, Tulsi, Diwali, Holi, Aarti, Monsoon, Sandhya, Forest, Navratri, Neel, Ratna, Prabhat, Mehndi, Haldi, Himalaya, Uttarayan, Mogra, or one's own; every one but Black and White with a header in its colour, a band of its colours under it, and its own colours for headings and the chosen tab and row, Session 42; and, Session 44, four hues at least: the header and the panels' title strips a static gradient, each column and the layer bar a zone in a colour of its own, `src/shared/appearance/zones.ts`, and the layer buttons in theirs); the phone pages are Black, fixed (`body.web`, Session 41: never Main's appearance) and the audience screens keep their own look (`body.output`), checked picture for picture against an earlier build (`scripts/audience-pixels.mjs`) and across every appearance (`tests/e2e/appearance.spec.ts`). **Back** and **Next** sit under LIVE (`NextBack.tsx`): Next is the one primary in a show and does what → does; **Clear all** is a strong secondary in its place with F1. Dialogs, menus and notices arrive and leave with motion tokens (`ui/motion.ts`), Calm, Lively or Off as the Appearance window sets it; show controls, thumbnails and the live picture never move, and with reduced motion nothing does. **The first start** (once per library, `welcome` in its settings): the lamp lights, then **Show on** the display free for the hall (Session 44, the primary when there is one), Set Up Screens…, Try a practice show first (`practice/`: placeholder slides on the operator's screen only, never sent to the main process) or Skip for now (the middle's three steps, `StartHere.tsx`: since Session 51 centred while they fit and scrolling from their top when they do not, as every empty place in the middle does, `CentredPlace` in `ui/States.tsx`, so in a 1024 × 645 window none of them is under the layer bar or the status bar; a step's buttons sit beside its words, or under them where there is no room for both). The practice show's **Try the keys** scrolls in a short window too. Default group colours are teal for Chorus and indigo for Verse, with no red (`shared/group-colors.ts`). A picture's sharpened copy is made at the size the largest audience screen draws it (`copySize`).

**Shown or hidden** (Session 26a). The Shastra tab, the Templates beside the playlists (with a playlist's Add a slot… and Save as template…) and the kirtan filters show only with **View › Show Shastra, Templates and Kirtan Details** (an admin's, off to begin with, the library setting `showExtras`; `IPC.library.setExtras`, `src/renderer/src/library/extras-store.ts`). Hidden, not deleted: their code, data and tests stay (tests turn it on with `showExtras` in `tests/e2e/helpers.ts`), as do the remote's library and the API that name a passage. **Import…** (Files…, A Folder…, From a Link…) is a labelled button under the search box, with New… and (when shown) the filters' button; Files… offers `importableExtensions()` (`src/main/import/scan.ts`), every extension the importer reads. **The Media list** (Session 26a, migration 37) shows only media `listed`: what was imported as media (a picture, video or sound on its own, or one a presentation from another program uses), not the pages Drashti drew from a deck (`source_kind 'drashti'`, `#page=` in the path); the same picture imported on its own lists the shared row. Deleting and restoring media is an admin's with roles on; Simple Mode refuses all of it.

**Kirtan details.** In **Kirtan**, **Details**: the category (Arti, Dhun, Prarthana, Stuti, Thal, Kirtan, Ashtak, Shlok, Bal sabha, Kishore sabha, Yuvak sabha, and any the mandir adds with **+**), the kavi and the raag (the ones already in the library are offered as you type), the occasions it is sung for (any number: type one and press Enter, **×** takes one off; festivals are offered), and its recording, chosen from the media library (a sound, or a video for its sound; **▶** plays it on the audio layer, F5 clears it). **Save** keeps them as one change for Undo. The **filter** button beside the search box opens filters by category, kavi, raag and occasion, each offering the values the library's kirtans have: the list then shows only the kirtans with every one chosen, says how many, and **Clear filters** shows everything again. Typing a kavi's or raag's name (or a category or occasion) in search finds their kirtans, and the result says where it matched ("Kavi: …").

**Making transliteration.** In **Kirtan**, **Transliteration** makes a kirtan's transliteration track from each slide's Gujarati lines (or its Hindi lines, on a slide with no Gujarati), by rule (`src/shared/translit.ts`), in the style chosen there: **Plain (no accent marks)**, the default ("Swami", "bhagwan", "darshan"), or **With accent marks (ISO 15919)** ("svāmī", "bhagvān", "darśan"); Drashti remembers the choice. Words read as they are said: the "a" each consonant carries is dropped at the end of a word ("ghar") and in the middle where it is not said ("bachpan", "akshardham"), and kept after a cluster ending in y, r, l, v or a nasal ("satya", "mitra", "dharma"). The two scripts differ where they are said differently: ઋ is "ru" and જ્ઞ "gn" in Gujarati, ऋ "ri" and ज्ञ "gy" in Hindi. The lines it makes go after the words they read, in the transliteration look, and stay editable: in Edit words, By language, they say **Made by Drashti**, and a line you change is yours from then on. Making it again (after changing the Gujarati, or the style) makes Drashti's own lines again and leaves lines you changed alone, unless you say otherwise: when any differ from what it would make, it asks first (**Keep my lines** or **Replace them**). A line you typed that says just what it would make counts as made. One Undo puts everything back. Rules get wrong: compounds and Sanskrit names where a middle "a" is said ("sahjanand" for Sahajanand, "upnishad"), names spelt with an "a" people no longer say ("gujrat"), nasal vowels in plain letters ("ganv" for gaon), and loanwords ending in a cluster ("filma"); the table of words it is tested on is in `src/shared/translit.test.ts`. The importers and Edit words use it too: a Latin line that reads like a Gujarati or Hindi line on its slide is its transliteration, even with no accent marks.

**Each screen's languages.** In **Screens**, each group has **A kirtan's languages on these screens**: **All, in each slide's order** (as before), or **Only these, in this order**, with a tick for each language and **Up** and **Down** arrows to order them. For example Audience shows Gujarati then transliteration, the stage Gujarati only, and a stream screen transliteration then English. Only a kirtan's slides change: in each text box the lines in other languages go, the rest come in the group's order and close up with no blank line where a language was, and shrink-to-fit fits what is left. When a slide's text boxes stand in one column, one left empty is taken out and the others close up around the column's middle. Text with no language (lines without letters, words in a legacy font), slides that are not a kirtan's, and a text box marked **On every screen, whatever its languages** in the slide editor (a title or a footer) always show in full. The live and next previews show a kirtan's slide as the first audience group shows it, and say so under the live picture; the stage preview follows the first stage group; slide thumbnails always show every language (`src/shared/language-view.ts`; since Session 11 the choice is kept in each Look: Looks, above).

**The stage screen.** In **Screens**, set a group to show **The stage view (performers)** instead of the audience picture. Its screens then show, in large text on black: the slide on the screens (**Now**), the slide Next will bring (**Next**, or the next picture or sound, or **End**), the slide's notes when it has any, and the clock. Backgrounds, pictures and videos are never drawn there (since Session 50 a stage layout's audience picture box is the one place a stage screen shows the hall's picture).
