The words of **Now** and **Next** start at the size they always had for their length and are measured to fit (Sessions 31–36, SLD-7, `StageFit` in `render/StageView.tsx`): a slide too long for its box is made smaller, never larger, until its last line shows (`data-stage-fit` says how far; `tests/e2e/stage.spec.ts`), and words that fit are drawn exactly as before (the pixel check's stage frames are identical). One slide is drawn alike everywhere: `tests/e2e/parity.spec.ts` finds a slide's shrunk words the same size (`data-fit`, to 3 decimals) and in the same lines on the hall's output, in the slide editor, on its thumbnail and on a phone's remote. In **Stage screen**, under the live picture (it shows a small copy of what the stage screens show), a message for the stage across the top of every stage screen (for example "Two minutes left"); the audience screens never show it, and **Clear all** leaves it, so it goes only with its own **Clear**. Layouts of one's own are made in the stage layout editor (Stage layouts, below).

**Props.** A prop is a logo or a fixed line of words (the mandir's name, say) that stays up over whatever slide is live. Under the live picture, **Props** lists them: **Show** puts one up and **Hide** takes it down (it stays up through Next, and through clearing the slide); **Clear props** (F4) takes them all down. **New prop** makes one from words (size, colour, along the top or the bottom, left, centre or right) or from a picture or video in the library (a corner and a size). Props are laid out on a 1920 × 1080 canvas, and each screen scales them as it does slides. Importing a ProPresenter 6 `Props.pro6` or a ProPresenter 7 `Configuration/Props` file brings its props in (importing it again replaces them); **×** deletes one.

**Messages.** Under the live picture, **New message** (in **Messages**) makes a template: a name, and its words with fields in braces, for example "Car {plate} please move". Each field is typed in when the message is shown, or can show a timer live (choose the timer for the field). To show one, fill in its fields and press **Show**: it goes across the bottom of the audience screens, next to any others already up. **Update** replaces it with what is in the fields now, **Take off** removes it, and **Clear messages** (F6) removes them all. Stage screens never show these messages.

**Timers.** Under the live picture, **New timer** (in **Timers**) makes a countdown (with its length, for example 5:00), a count-up, a countdown to a time of day (for example 19:30), or a clock. Countdowns can keep counting below zero (shown as -0:12) or stop at 0:00. **Start**, **Pause** and **Reset** (or **Stop** for the time of day and the clock); **Edit** changes a timer, even while it runs. **Show on the screens** (Session 50) puts the timer up alone, in Drashti's **Timer only** layout, in the slide's place over whatever background is up (the engine's `showTimer`: a slide layer with `timer`, its presentation id `timer:<id>` and its words in the library's default theme; `src/shared/timer-slides.ts`), coming on with Drashti's default transition (a fade of half a second when that is a cut); **Take off the screens** or Clear slide (F2) takes it off, going as it came, and Clear all at once; Next goes on in the playlist from where the show was. Nothing is added to the library. LIVE says "Timer · <name>" with Take off, a stage screen's current-slide box shows its time live, a phone names it ("Timer: <name>") with Clear, and after an unexpected stop it comes back over the slide. Until Session 50 it went across the bottom as a message ("Sabha starts in 4:59"), as a playlist item's timers still show one. Stage screens show every timer that is running or paused. Only start, pause and reset travel from the engine to the windows; each window works out the time from the clock the windows share, so every screen turns over within a tenth of a second of the others and nothing is sent each second. Timers are kept in the library; whether they are running is not kept over a restart.

**Editing words.** **Edit words**, above the slides, opens the presentation's words as plain text, in the same format lyrics files are imported in: a line in square brackets such as `[Verse 1]` or `[Chorus]` starts a group, a blank line starts a new slide, and a header again with nothing under it repeats that group (the presentation then plays in that "As written" order). Fix words, add or remove slides and groups, or move a group by moving its block, and **Save** (or **Cmd+Enter** on macOS, **Ctrl+Enter** on Windows). Groups are matched by name, and slides by their words, so a slide whose words you changed keeps its look, its background and its cues; a new slide looks like the first slide of its group (or the default look, in a new group). Slides without words (a picture, a blank slide) and hidden slides are not in the text and stay where they are. Arrangements keep the groups that are still there. If the slide on the screens changed, the screens show the new words at once. **Undo** (at the foot of the left column, or **Edit > Undo**) puts the words back as they were. Words typed in a legacy Gujarati or Hindi font open read-only, with the font named, because saving them as plain text would garble them. **New…**, beside the search box, opens the same editor with a name: paste a kirtan's words, press **Make slides**, and it is a presentation in the library.

**Kirtans and their languages.** A kirtan's words come in up to four language tracks, Gujarati, Hindi, English (the meaning) and transliteration, slide by slide. They are not kept anywhere apart from the slides: each line on a slide has a language (its script decides Gujarati or Hindi; a Latin line is English or transliteration, as it was typed or imported), and a track is simply that language's lines (`src/shared/tracks.ts`). So Edit words, the slide editor, search and the screens always see the same words. **Kirtan**, above the slides, says whether the presentation is a kirtan, how many slides have each language, and has **Make it a kirtan** and **Not a kirtan** (each only adds or takes away the kirtan's details; the words never move; Undo puts it back). For a kirtan, **Edit words** has two views: **All words** (the plain text above, which adds and removes slides) and **By language**: every slide that plays, with its lines in one language, or in all of them side by side. A slide with no line in a language shows it as **Missing** (amber, dashed), not as an empty line; typing there adds it, in the look that language has elsewhere in the kirtan (or the theme's), after the languages that come before it on the slide. Changing a line keeps its look; taking a language's lines away closes up the rest. Only the lines that changed are rewritten, as one change for Undo. A Latin line changed in All words stays the language it was, so plain transliteration (with no accent marks) never turns into English. Copying a slide copies its lines in every language. In the slide editor, a text box with lines in several languages shows **Several** as its language.

**Editing slides.** **Edit slides**, beside **Edit words** (or a double-click on a slide), opens the slide editor over the operator window, at the slide on the screens (or the one a search found). The slides are on the left, the slide being edited in the middle (drawn by the same renderer as the screens), and what is selected on the right.

**Words the hall can read** (Sessions 31–36, SLD-1). Every text box is measured where it is drawn (`useShrinkToFit` in `render/SlideView.tsx`): one whose words run past it, unshrunk or shrunk as far as shrink-to-fit goes, gets `data-overset` and tells whoever asked through `MeasureContext` (`render/measure.tsx`): a thumbnail, in the slide grid and in the slide editor's list, shows a warning mark in its corner and adds "words run past a box" to its name, and the inspector says **Words run past the box** with what to do. The inspector also reads the size the box's smallest words are drawn at, at a 1080-line reference (their size × the fit × 1080 ÷ the slide's height, `src/shared/readable.ts`): a warning below 36 px, a note below 48 px. The floors are the editor's only: the screens never refuse a slide, and shrink-to-fit's own floor (`MIN_FIT`, a tenth of the size set) is unchanged. An import counts the slides that may run over or sit below 36 px by an estimate (shastra-slides' word widths; it has no window to measure in) and says so in its report. `tests/e2e/render.spec.ts` and `tests/e2e/editor.spec.ts` check the marks, and the pixel check has a shrink-to-fit frame (identical on both builds).

**Safe areas** (Sessions 31–36, SLD-2). **View › Show Safe Areas** (Pro Mode; off to begin with, kept with the library's settings as `showSafeAreas`) draws over the slide editor's slide (`editor/SafeAreas.tsx`): action-safe at 93% and title-safe at 90% (the HD standard), the ticker's band (its 7.5%, `TICKER_HEIGHT`, now in `src/shared/safe-areas.ts` with the message banner's size, which the screens draw by) and a message's band above it, and a line where each screen group's canvas, or its Fill, cuts the slide off (`cropLines` in `src/shared/scaling.ts`: a 4:3 slide filling a 16:9 group is cut at 96 and 672). A box whose words sit in the ticker's band on a hall group whose Look shows the ticker (`bottomBand`, as that group places the slide) gets **Words sit where the ticker runs** in the inspector, safe areas shown or not. There is no Look option to keep slides above the ticker: it would rescale them live whenever an announcement came or went.

**Darkening behind the words** (Sessions 31–36, SLD-3). A slide's own settings in the slide editor, and a theme, have **Darken behind the words**: None (as every slide has to begin with: migration 43 adds `slides.darken`, NULL for every slide), a band behind each box's words, or the whole picture under the words, 40, 60 or 80% dark (`Darken` in `src/shared/model.ts`; drawn by `SlideView` and the editor's canvas as `data-darken`, over the background, a video included, and under the words). It rides the engine state with its slide (`ENGINE_STATE_VERSION` 10: an older phone or node says the state is incompatible rather than leaving it out), is in the GPU warm-up's scenes, and has no motion of its own; Undo covers it, and applying a theme sets it on every slide. The inspector reads the words' contrast against what is behind them, darkening included (`wordsContrast` in `src/shared/contrast.ts`: a warning below 4.5:1, aiming for 7:1), and the theme editor its words' lowest; the theme contract counts it. `tests/e2e/darken.spec.ts` sees white over white warn and darkening clear it (and Undo bring it back), and on the hall's output a video at a fifth of its light under 80% with the words still white over it; the pixel check is identical with none chosen.

- **Select and move.** Click something to select it (Shift-click adds or takes away; drag across the empty slide to select several; with several selected, dragging any of them moves them all, and a click on one leaves it alone selected). Drag to move: it snaps to the slide's edges and middle and to the edges and middles of the other elements, and pink guides show where; hold **Alt** to place it freely. The square handles resize it (the corner opposite stays put, also when it is turned; pictures and videos keep their proportions, Shift lets them go), and the round handle above turns it (Shift for steps of 15 degrees; it holds at a quarter turn unless Alt is down).
- **The keyboard.** **Tab** selects the next element; the arrows move it a pixel (ten with **Shift**); **Enter** types in a text box; **Delete** removes; **Cmd/Ctrl+D** duplicates; **Esc** stops typing, then lets go of the selection, then closes.
- **Adding.** **Words** adds a text box in the presentation's theme (its place and style; what is typed takes each language's look), **Shape** a rectangle, rounded rectangle, ellipse or line, and **Picture or video** one from the library at its own proportions.
- **A Timer** (Session 50). **Timer** in the toolbar adds one of the timers, live, in its own box; the inspector sets which timer, its font, size, weight, colour, shadow, outline and alignment, a **last minute** colour (off to begin with; from 1:00 left), and for a countdown what happens **at zero**: hold at 0:00 (the default) or go on to the next slide. **New Timer Slide…**, beside New slide (and in a slide's right-click menu in the slide grid), makes a slide after it in one of two ready layouts, **Timer only** or **Timer with words** ("Starting in" until it is changed), in the presentation's theme, as one change for Undo (`src/shared/timer-slides.ts`, `editor/NewTimerSlide.tsx`). Every screen draws a Timer from the engine's timers (`render/TimerElementView.tsx`): once a second only the digits that changed move, a Calm Look fading each and a Bold one rolling it up (`render/timer-digits.ts`), and stage screens cut; where slides are drawn as lower thirds, a Timer's slide is drawn whole. The library keeps it as an element of kind `timer` (migration 46).
- **The inspector.** Place, size, turn and opacity as numbers; lining up (with each other, or one element with the slide), spacing out evenly, bringing forward and sending back, duplicating and deleting; for words, the language, font, size, weight, italic (not for Gujarati, Hindi or Sanskrit, always upright), colour, a shadow (none, Drashti's soft one, or its own colour, blur and offset), an outline round the letters, and for the whole box its alignment, line spacing and shrink-to-fit. Text styles go to the selected words while typing with words selected, otherwise to the whole box. Shapes have a fill, an outline and a corner radius; pictures and videos their fit, and a video its looping and sound.
- **A font not on this computer** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-1, `render/font-probe.ts`). `document.fonts.check()` says yes for any font it is not loading, there or not (`tests/e2e/fonts-missing.spec.ts` shows it), so each box's named fonts (its own and its runs', not Drashti's bundled Noto fonts) are measured instead: a sample drawn in the font over two fallbacks, off the page; a font the computer lacks measures exactly as each fallback. A box whose font is missing is marked `data-missing-font` wherever it is drawn (Main's outputs, a node's, the stream's page, thumbnails), drawing nothing different; outputs report it (`missingFonts`), so the operator sees **Font not on this computer: <name>** beside LIVE and on the screens dashboard's card (**Font not on this node** for a node's display).
- **Gujarati and Hindi upright** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-2). Gujarati and Devanagari letters slanted or spaced apart break at their joins, so they are drawn upright with no letter-spacing whatever a file stored (`standsUpright` in `src/shared/text-runs.ts`, used by `runStyle` in `render/SlideView.tsx`). A run's letters decide, not the language it is marked as, so a transliteration line in the same box keeps its italic; words in a legacy Gujarati or Hindi font stand upright too. What was stored is kept, and an import's report counts the slides this applies to. The inspector offers no Italic for a box or words all in Gujarati, Hindi or Sanskrit, and warns of a box with Gujarati or Hindi in it whose line spacing is below 1.3 (`INDIC_LINE_HEIGHT_MIN`). New words get 1.35 (`NEW_TEXT_LINE_HEIGHT` in `src/shared/model.ts`: a new box or prop, lyrics, a new library's themes, the setup wizard's and practice slides), and under a theme at least 1.35 (new slides, and a new presentation's words: `styleNewText` in `src/main/library/themes.ts`); stored themes never change (Shastra passages are made from them), so applying one gives its own, and slides already made keep theirs. `tests/e2e/render.spec.ts` draws an imported italic, letter-spaced Gujarati line upright, and `tests/e2e/editor.spec.ts` sees the warning.
- **Words say their language** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-4, `render/lang.ts`). A phone's reader, its fonts and a browser's translator follow `lang`, so words carry it wherever they are drawn: a slide's runs and boxes (as before), a message and each of the ticker's announcements, the stage's message and notes (a stage layout's too), the remote's notes and search, the library's search, Shastra's snippets, Edit words' fields and the announcement box. Words no one marked go by their letters (`textLang`); the Kirtan dialog's rows are English and are left alone. Transliteration says what it transliterates: `gu-Latn`, `hi-Latn` or `sa-Latn`, from the slide's first Gujarati, Hindi or Sanskrit line (`translitSource` in `src/shared/tracks.ts`; a screen's copy without those lines keeps it as `translitOf`), `gu-Latn` when nothing says. The outputs, the stream's page, the stage display and the remote are `translate="no"`, so no browser machine-translates a kirtan's words. `tests/e2e/lang.spec.ts` sees each, and every tag passes axe's valid-lang (`expectValidLang`); the pixel check has a message frame, identical on both builds.
- **Cutting words** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-5). Words cut to a limit (a name, a snippet, a slide's words on import, a message) go through `cutText` in `src/shared/text.ts`: at most so many UTF-16 units, what every limit counts, and never inside a letter, so a Gujarati or Hindi conjunct (ક્ષ, क्ष), a vowel sign or an emoji stays whole or goes whole (`Intl.Segmenter`'s letters; an older phone's browser without it backs off over marks and viramas, `cutByMarks`). The lint rule `drashti/text-cut` (`tools/eslint/text-cut.mjs`) refuses `.slice(0, n)`, `.substring` or `.substr` on a string anywhere in `src/` (the type decides: an array's slice is fine); ids and codes (a colour, a time, a hash, a pairing code) and cuts at a character the code found (an extension's dot, a newline) are allowed by name in `eslint.config.mjs`, each with why. `src/main/text-cut-lint.test.ts` plants one in each process's code.
- **Fonts for both scripts** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-7). Noto Sans comes without its own Devanagari: `render/fonts.css` declares its other faces itself, fontsource's own in their order and with their unicode ranges (a subset's CSS file alone has no range, and would claim every letter), so Hindi and Sanskrit are drawn by Noto Sans Devanagari wherever they appear: a message, the ticker, the stage's message and notes, the operator window. Every stack but transliteration's has both Indian scripts, after Latin so Latin letters are drawn as before (`LANG_FONT_STACK` in `render/fonts.ts`): a Hindi word in a Gujarati line is drawn by Noto Sans Devanagari, a Gujarati one in a Hindi line by Noto Sans Gujarati. `tests/e2e/render.spec.ts` asks Chromium which font drew each (a Hindi message, the ticker, the stage's message, Devanagari inside a Gujarati line); the pixel check is identical. ISO 15919's three combining marks (U+0310, U+0325, U+035F) are in no bundled font yet; ISO transliteration is chosen by the operator, never the default.
- **Hidden characters** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-3). `scripts/unicode-check.mjs` reads every file git tracks and reports a direction override or isolate (a line that reads one way and runs another), a zero-width character or a filler, with where it is; it never changes a file. ZWJ and ZWNJ pass only between Indian letters, as a half-form is written (`tests/fixtures/half-forms.txt`); code writes them as `\u200d` and `\u200c` (the RTF reader's and search's were literals until then). CI's checks job runs it after its `--self-check` (a planted U+202E and a ZWJ between Latin letters found, a half-form passed); `src/main/release-checks/unicode-check.test.ts` tests it.
- **Text in one form** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-6, `src/shared/nfc.ts`). Text is put in NFC where it enters: an import (`toNewPresentation`, and Shastra texts and calendars), a slide editor save, Edit words (a save, or a new presentation from words), a kirtan's tracks, details and categories, a made transliteration, a quote, a phone's message fields and announcement. Never in the database layer, which theme apply and Undo also use, and never words typed in a legacy font (their letters are codes for that font). So क़ is stored one way whether it came as U+0958 or as U+0915 U+093C, and comparisons (Edit words lining up slides, a transliteration still as Drashti made it) normalise both sides (`sameText`). `src/main/import/pipeline.test.ts` and `tests/e2e/nfc.spec.ts` (an import, Edit words and the slide editor) see it.
- **Clocks in Main's time** (Sessions 31–36, I18N-8). Times and lengths are written by one module, `src/shared/format.ts` (`formatClock`, `formatDuration`, the `ClockStyle` of a locale and a time zone); timers write theirs through it (`timerDuration`, `formatTimer`). Main's style (`mainClockStyle` in `src/main/clock-style.ts`: its locale and time zone) goes to every output in its context, to each node in the welcome Main sends it (checked there, `validClockStyle`), to the stream's page in its context, and, as before, to the stage display and the remote; each page gives it to what it draws (`ClockStyleContext` in `render/clock-style.ts`), so the stage clock and a clock timer show Main's time on a node in another time zone. A style a computer cannot write in falls back to its own way rather than no clock. `tests/e2e/clocks.spec.ts` stands Main in Pacific/Chatham and sees a node's stage screen show Main's time. Gujarati digits are not used yet.
- **Typing in place** uses ProseMirror (`src/renderer/src/editor/`, MIT, `LICENSES/editor/`). Gujarati and Hindi input methods work as in any text field; pasted text comes in as plain text. Each run's look travels with its words, so a box's runs come back exactly as they were where nothing changed. Words typed in a legacy font can be moved, resized and turned, but not edited.
- **Slides and groups.** Above the slides: a new slide after the one being edited, a new group, duplicate, move up or down (on into the next group at the end of one), and delete; thumbnails can be dragged to another place or group. With nothing selected, the inspector shows the slide: its label, its group (or a new one), hidden in the show, a colour behind it, its group's name and colour (or deleting the group with its slides), its background picture or video and its sound (cues: they go on the background and audio layers when it goes live), its notes for the stage screens, how it comes on (the presentation's transition, a cut, or a dissolve of so many seconds) and whether it moves on by itself after so many seconds; then the presentation's transition and whether moving on by itself loops back to the first slide; **Give every slide this slide's look** (its colour, its shapes, and the place and style of its words, each language in its look; words never change; one step for Undo); **Make a theme from this slide**; and Drashti's own default transition for presentations without one, a cut until it is changed (it changes at once). Other cues a slide has (clears, messages) are kept as they are.
- **Undo and Redo** inside the editor (the buttons, **Cmd/Ctrl+Z**, **Cmd+Shift+Z** or **Ctrl+Y**; a drag is one step, and arrow-key moves close together are one step). While typing they undo the typing.
- **Save** (or **Cmd/Ctrl+S**) writes the slides as one change: the slide on the screens shows it at once, and **Undo** in the operator window puts all of it back. Saving keeps everything that did not change exactly as stored (a moved element changes only its place), and data Drashti does not know about inside an element stays with it. If the presentation changed somewhere else while it was open (an import, Edit words, a theme), Drashti asks before saving over that. **Cancel**, or closing with changes, asks first, and keeps nothing.

**Themes.** **Themes**, in the header, lists the library's themes, each as a preview with a placeholder line in each language; **Drashti default** is the one presentations made in Drashti start with until another is made the default (**Make it the default for new presentations**; the words editor's new slides use a presentation's theme too). Ten **built-in themes** come with every library once (Session 26, `src/shared/builtin-themes.ts`; a deleted one stays deleted, migration 40 marks them): Midnight, Plum and Flame, Saffron, Maroon and Gold, Forest, Ocean, Ivory, Charcoal, High Contrast White on Black and High Contrast Black on White, each a colour behind the words, Drashti's bundled fonts, and a shadow behind light words. Every language's words and the reference line are at least 7:1 against the background (WCAG's AAA, for dim halls and washed-out projectors; `src/shared/builtin-themes.test.ts` measures every one). Saving a built-in theme makes a copy; the built-in one stays as it is. Every theme is held to **the theme contract** (Sessions 31–36, SLD-4, `src/shared/theme-contract.ts`, written in `docs/design.md` §37): words at least 36 px at 1080 lines, at least 4.5:1 against the background, weight 400 or more, lines at least 1.15 apart, and the box inside title-safe; the theme editor says what a theme misses as it is changed, and saving it saves it all the same, saying what it misses (`warnings`). A theme sets, for English, Gujarati, Hindi and transliteration each, the font (empty uses Drashti's bundled font for that language), size (for a 1080-high slide; other sizes scale), weight, colour and shadow; where the first text box on each slide goes and how its text is aligned; and the background: left as it is, a colour behind every slide, or a picture or video from the library, which becomes a background cue on every slide (so it is up wherever you go live, and carries on from slide to slide). The preview shows a line in each language. To apply a theme, mark presentations in the library (click one, Cmd/Ctrl-click to add more) and press **Apply to …**, or choose a playlist and press **Apply to its presentations** (decks, whose pages are pictures, are left as they are): each line takes its language's look, the words never change, text typed in a legacy font keeps its font, and a live slide changes at once. **Undo** puts every one of them back. On a presentation from the **Templates** library, **Make a theme from this** (above its slides) makes a theme from its first text box: its place, alignment, each language's look as it has it, and its first slide's background.

**Searching.** Type in the box above the library (**Cmd+F** on macOS, **Ctrl+F** on Windows, goes there): results come as you type, from titles and from the words on the slides, in any of the four languages. Each word you type can be the start of a word ("nam" finds "Namūnā"), and Latin accents do not matter ("namuna" finds "Namūnā"). Transliteration's many spellings find each other: v and w, a doubled vowel and its single one ("Shree" and "Shri"), x and ksh ("Axar" and "Akshar"), f and ph, and ru and ri after a consonant ("Krushna" and "Krishna"; "guru" and "giri" stay apart), also when a word is typed only partly ("aks" finds "Axar"). A result shows the line that matched; opening it (a click, or Enter for the first) selects the presentation and brings that slide into view. **Esc** empties the box. Text typed in a legacy Gujarati or Hindi font cannot be searched until a converter for that font exists: the results say how many presentations have such text, with **Show them** to list them (their titles are still searched).

**Running a sabha from a playlist.** Click an item in the open playlist to see it in the middle: a presentation's slides in the item's order, or the picture, video or sound. Click a slide (or press Space) to start; Next and Previous then go along the presentation and on into the next item, or back to the previous item's last slide, stepping over headers, placeholders and anything missing. A picture or video item goes up as the background and takes the slide off, as a video input item does since Session 51 (over its fixed second; one this computer does not have is stepped over: see "A video input as the background"); a sound item plays on the audio layer and leaves the picture as it is. **Shift+→** and **Shift+←** jump to the first slide of the next or previous item. The live item has a red dot, and the middle follows the show from item to item (unless you have clicked away to look at something else). Each presentation item can play in its own arrangement: choose it above its slides; **As the presentation plays** follows the presentation's own choice. Under the live picture, **Next** shows what Next will put up, and every output loads that slide's images and videos ahead, so they appear with its text.

**Backgrounds.** A slide with a background image or video (or, since Session 51, a video input, by its name) puts it on the background layer when it goes live. It stays up on later slides without a background of their own. A later slide with the same file lets it carry on playing; a different file replaces it, and the old picture stays until the new one has its first frame, so the screens never flash black. **Clear background** (F3) removes it and leaves the text. Videos loop, or play once and hold their last frame, as the slide says. A window that opens late (or the live preview after a reload) starts the video where the others are. If a file cannot play, it still goes up: the screens show no background and the live preview says so ("Background cannot play"); a video input this computer does not have leaves the background as it was, and LIVE says so. **A missing file behind a slide** (Session 52, `missingFile` in `src/main/engine/show-engine.ts`) does as a missing video input does: a background cue whose file is missing (not found at import, or deleted from the library, as the cue query reads it) puts nothing up, the background stays as it was (never black because of it), and the slide layer names the file (`SlideLayer.fileMissing`; what comes next too, `UpNext.fileMissing`); before, it went up all the same and every screen went black. LIVE says "File missing: “…”. The background stays as it was." under its picture in `warning-fg`, in Pro and Simple Mode (`operator/LivePreview.tsx`); the slide's thumbnail shows the library's Missing badge instead of its still (`CueBackground` in `operator/SlideGrid.tsx`); the slide editor says "The file “…” is missing: the background stays as it was when this slide goes up." for a file Media lists as missing (`editor/SlidePanel.tsx`); and Simple Mode's picture of what is next adds ", over the file “…”, which is missing: the background stays as it is" (`operator/NextPreview.tsx`). Restart recovery says it again for the slide it puts back. Found again (**Find missing media…** in the import report, or the same file imported again), or made again by a conversion, it goes up behind its slide the next time the slide goes up: the engine reads every presentation's slides again after an import or a conversion (`slides.invalidate()` in `src/main/index.ts`; before, an import read again only the presentations it replaced, and a conversion only those it moved). A playlist item whose file is missing still says so, and Next goes past it (`tests/e2e/missing-background-file.spec.ts`; `src/main/engine/missing-files.test.ts`).

**Transitions.** Each slide comes on with its own transition, else its presentation's, else Drashti's default (a cut until it is changed; all three are set in the slide editor). A dissolve fades the old slide out as the new one comes in, the new one added light for light over the old (`plus-lighter`), so the picture never dips and nothing goes black; a background picture or video that the slide brings dissolves with it, and one that carries on from the slide before just carries on. The fade is timed from the moment the engine put the slide up, so every screen is at the same point. It starts once the new slide's pictures and videos can be drawn (the old slide stays up meanwhile; the next slide's media is loaded ahead anyway). A screen that opens or reloads after it has finished shows the finished slide. The stage screens always cut, and clearing and black-out are immediate. Recovery after a restart puts the slide back without a fade.

**The kinds, and Movement** (Sessions 26d and 27; `src/renderer/src/render/transition-frame.ts`, `reveal.ts`). Cut, **Fade** (eased since Session 27: smoothstep on Calm screens, Bold's own ease in and out on Bold ones), **Fade through black**, **Slide** and **Slow zoom** (Repeat Slides' own, drawn the same whatever the Movement), and the design lab's: **Wipe** (a hard diagonal edge crosses the words), **Push** (the words go out to the left as the new come in) and **Zoom** (the words grow away as the new grow into place), which move the words only on screens whose Look is **Bold** and fade elsewhere; and **Line by line** and **Word by word** (the words come one line, or one word, after another, rising on Bold screens), in both Movements, though never word by word on a kirtan's slides (they fade instead). Words are cut as each line's language cuts them (`Intl.Segmenter`), so a Gujarati or Devanagari letter cluster is never split. While any of these moves the words, the slide's pictures, shapes and colour, and the background behind, only dissolve, so a darshan picture stays whole; afterwards the slide is drawn exactly as designed. Movement never changes a transition's length: the design lab's timings are fitted into the slide's own. An older Drashti opening a library or backup that uses one of Session 27's kinds reads it as Drashti's default transition (`src/shared/model-schema.ts`). **Lower thirds** (a Look's and the stream's Camera layout's) come in, change as their box holds, and leave with a short fade (Calm 180 ms, Bold 310 ms) when their slide is taken off on its own; the engine records the slide taken off and when (`state.slideOff`), so every screen fades it from the same moment. Clear all takes it off at once. The idle rotation's dissolve is eased too, the item up going out as the next comes in, and a prop's or the logo's videos play from when it went up (`shownAt`), so a screen that opens later plays them where the others are. Before a screen first draws the show it draws every kind once in both Movements, unseen (black), so the GPU has made what they need and the first Bold transition of a sabha does not stutter (`src/renderer/src/render/WarmUp.tsx`; `docs/design.md`, section 40).

Dissolves cut short (Session 23, `src/renderer/src/render/background-slots.ts`, `SlideLayerView.tsx`): a slide that brings another background while a background is still dissolving in used to leave that one on screen part-way through (a third of the way, say: a dim picture) until the next was ready, and then jump back up before fading. Now the dissolve on screen carries on to its end while the next background loads, and the next one dissolves in from it once it is ready and the first has finished (or simply replaces it, if its own dissolve is over by then). A slide that was still waiting for its pictures when the next slide came had never been seen, so the slide on screen stays the one going away (before, the unseen slide could flash up whole). A screen that opens or reloads part-way through a dissolve shows the slide and its background whole, never fading the background in from black. `tests/e2e/dissolves.spec.ts` checks the first and the last.

**Auto-advance.** A slide set to move on by itself (in the slide editor) does so after its time, in play order, with its transition and cues as if Next had been pressed. On the last slide it goes back to the first when the presentation loops, and otherwise stays; it never goes on into the next playlist item. Under the live picture (in Pro Mode and Simple Mode) the time left counts down. Anything that puts another slide up (Next, Previous, a click, Back, Put it back) starts that slide's own count; clearing the slide stops it; black-out and the logo leave it counting; editing the live slide keeps its count with the new time. While a slide counts, what is live is saved again every second, so after an unexpected stop it carries on with the time it had left.

**Music (audio playlists)** (Session 14, `src/shared/music.ts`). The **Music** panel in the live column (under **Audio**) keeps lists of sounds from the media library to play one after another on the audio layer, apart from the slides (music before the sabha, say). Since Session 44 Audio's **+** has exactly two choices, **Create playlist** and **Import** (the system's dialog for sounds, into the audio playlist shown, or a new one); sound files dragged from the Finder or Explorer, and sounds dragged from the library's sheet, go into the audio playlist they are dropped on (only sounds go in: anything else dropped is imported into the library, and Drashti says so); the audio playlists are rows, the chosen one's sounds below it, with Rename and Delete on its row, and several can be chosen and deleted together with one Undo. **Add sounds…** ticks sounds from the library; the arrows reorder and **×** takes one out. A playlist made from a folder (a smart playlist, or a Dropbox folder link's) that holds sounds also gets an audio playlist named after it with " - Audios" (Session 44, `src/main/music/folder-audios.ts`): the same sounds of the library in the playlist's order, nothing imported twice, kept in step as the playlist changes (a sound that joins it joins at the end, one that leaves it leaves; one the operator takes out stays out). **Play** starts the list (or a click on a track starts there); **Pause** stops it where it is and **Play** goes on from there; previous and next move between tracks; the loop button goes round again at the end (on to begin with; off, it stops after the last), and the shuffle button plays the tracks in a shuffled order from the next Play (a track clicked plays first). Since Session 51 one row holds them all (`music/MusicPanel.tsx`): previous, Play or Pause, next, then Back 15 s, Forward 15 s and Last 10 s (below) as small icon buttons named by their tooltips and read out, as previous and next are (with their words the row would not fit the live column, about 345 px inside its panel in a 1024 × 645 window, where the column scrolls), then loop and shuffle, which go to a line of their own first when the column is dragged narrower. The engine keeps the tracks in play order and moves on as each ends, from its start time and length (learned as the audio player loads it), so the audio player, the remote and recovery agree; a track that cannot play is passed after 20 s. The one audio player plays it, never the screens, with a short fade in as each track starts and out as it stops (pause, next, Clear audio). Moving on to the next track, and starting, pausing or moving through the music, never stop Back or Put it back from working. How it meets the rest of the audio layer (one sound at a time):

- a slide's sound cue, a sound item or a kirtan's recording takes the audio layer, and the music stops with its fade; Back after that Next brings it back where it would be by now;
- **Clear audio** (F5) and **Clear all** stop it; **Put it back** brings it back where it would be by now (tracks that would have ended meanwhile passed);
- music playing now is never undone by Back or Put it back (it is independent of the slides);
- after an unexpected stop it comes back where it would be by now, or paused where it was.
  Simple Mode has **Play music** and **Pause music** (for the list played last), the remote's More tab plays, pauses and moves to the next track, and scripts use `POST /api/v1/music/play`, `/pause`, `/next` and `/previous`. A stage layout's "time left" box holds still while it is paused. Changing the lists is the operator's (Simple Mode refuses).

**Playback markers** (Session 14, `src/shared/markers.ts`). A video or a sound in the media list has a **Markers** button (a bookmark): a silent preview, **Start** and **End** (empty for the beginning and the end; **Here** takes the time the preview shows), and named markers (**Add at the time shown**; a click on one shows it in the preview). They are kept with the media item, so every slide, item and theme that uses the file follows them: it starts at its start point and stops (or, looping, goes round again) at its end point, on every screen, the audio player and the stream's own sound alike, and drift correction's loop is the part between them. Since Session 52 so does a video placed on a slide, a prop's and the logo's, which played the whole file before: the engine stamps their files' points, as the library has them, on the slide layer and each prop (`SlideLayer.clips` and `PropItem.clips`, by media id, read with `videoClip` in `src/shared/engine/state.ts`; `clipsOf` in `show-engine.ts`), however it goes up (Next, an edit while live, Back, Put it back, restart recovery) and never from a page's command, so every screen, node, key and fill and the stream play the same part (`SlideVideo` in `render/SlideView.tsx` plays by `playback.ts`'s timing, as a background does), and a slide video's sound plays it too, in the audio player and the stream's own sound (`soundsOf` in `audio/sounds.ts`). Set to loop, it goes round between them; played once, it stops at its end point and holds there; points saved while it plays move it there at once, on every screen (`refreshMarkers`). They have no marker buttons and no Back 15 s or Forward 15 s (only the background and the sound have them), and a video from Media in the picture-in-picture box still plays the whole file (`tests/e2e/markers.spec.ts`, `tests/e2e/prop-videos.spec.ts`; `src/main/engine/video-clips.test.ts`). Played once, it stops within two frames of its end point (Session 15): the stop is timed from where each copy plays, not left to the next quarter-second check, which let it run up to a quarter of a second past. While a file with markers plays as the background or on the audio layer, its markers show as buttons under the live picture; a press jumps every screen and the audio player there together, from the engine's clock (a jump never stops Back or Put it back from working). The remote's More tab has the same buttons, and scripts use `POST /api/v1/markers/{id}/jump`. Imported presentations bring their videos' and sounds' start and end points and markers with them, when the file has none yet; the older formats' markers follow the community notes on them and are not yet checked against real files, so the import report asks to look them over. Setting them is the operator's (Simple Mode refuses). **Back 15 s**, **Forward 15 s** and **Last 10 s** (Session 50, `src/main/engine/skip.ts`, `markers/SkipButtons.tsx`), as on the mandir's audio controls, sit beside the marker jumps under the live picture (for the background video and a single sound), and in Music in the row with Previous, Play/Pause and Next, as icons since Session 51 (for an audio playlist's track). Each is a jump as to a marker (the engine's `skip`), in step on every screen, the nodes, the stream and the audio player, and never past the file's start or end points: Back within the first 15 s goes to the start, Forward within the last 15 s to the end, and Last 10 s on a file (or the part between its points) shorter than 10 s to the end, where an audio playlist goes on to its next track; while paused, it moves where playing will start. Since Session 51 a sound sent somewhere new by one of them or a marker lands in step however quickly they are pressed, and Back (or a marker) from a video held at its own end plays again at once, in step on every screen (see "Sound"). They are dimmed until the file's length is known (a moment after it starts). Pro Mode only: Simple Mode, the phone remote and the keys have none (`tests/e2e/skips.spec.ts`).

**Images and videos on slides** draw on the outputs and in the live preview where the slide places them; a video plays from when its slide goes live (since Session 52 between its file's start and end points: see "Playback markers"). Slide thumbnails show images, and a still frame for each video (with the slide's background behind its text): thumbnails never play video. Drashti makes a video's still frame the first time a thumbnail needs it and keeps it in the media folder.

**Sound.** One hidden audio player plays every sound: a slide's audio cue (on the audio layer, at the cue's volume, looping or not as the cue says, and carrying on through later slides until **Clear audio** (F5) or another sound replaces it), and the sound of the background video and of videos on the live slide, each in step with the picture. The header shows what is on the audio layer, and slide thumbnails mark the slides that start a sound. The screens and the live preview are always silent. Choose where the sound goes (usually the mixer) under **Screens > Sound output**; Drashti remembers the choice. If that output is not connected when Drashti starts (or goes away during a show), sound plays on the system default and a warning stays in the status bar until it is back (the status bar always says where the sound goes). Every screen showing the same video shows the same frame: all windows follow one clock and correct any drift (by playing a few percent faster or slower, or jumping when far out), and a screen that opens late or reloads joins where the others are. A loop goes round by a jump aimed ahead, as every jump is, never by the element's own loop, which came round late (a video's sound by up to 0.3 s, so it fell further behind its picture each time round); a sound or a picture still out of step once it has played on after a jump, or after it starts, is aimed again at once rather than left to catch up a few percent at a time (Sessions 31–36). Since Session 51 a sound the engine sends somewhere new (a skip, a jump to a marker) is aimed afresh (`resync` in `render/playback.ts`): a landing or a start not yet measured, or a seek still landing, is never measured from the new place, where it would seem 15 or 30 s off and teach the sound a lead at its 0.5 s cap; the jump has re-aims of its own, whatever the landings before it used up; and a move shorter than the half second that makes a sound jump lands by a jump too, not eased at 2% a second (0.3 s took 15 s). Since Session 52 a screen's picture is aimed afresh the same way, every screen alike, once it has its first frame (before that its start is aimed by the clock and measured as a start is, so a file still loading is left to its start): after quick skips a screen's picture had landed up to 0.45 s ahead until its next check a quarter of a second on, or a seek's time behind, easing back at 5% a second, and now it lands in step; a move shorter than the quarter of a second that makes a picture jump lands by a jump too (0.2 s took 4 s); and a picture held at its end point that a marker sends a moment back plays that stretch with the sound, where it stayed frozen. Also since Session 52 the first jump of any sound or picture, aimed again, no longer overshoots: its seek's time was learned twice, as the seek's own and as how far off it then played, so the re-aim landed about as far ahead as the jump had landed behind (−0.100 s, then +0.095 s, with seeks of 0.1 s); now each jump keeps the lead it was aimed with (`aimed`), and its landing, once it has played on, teaches that less how far off it is. Nothing hunts: at most two re-aims in a row (`REAIMS`), none for drift, and loops as they were (`render/playback.test.ts`). And a file played once and held at its own end (`ended`), taken back by Back 15 s or a marker, plays from where it lands at once: until then its play() waited for the next quarter-second check, up to a quarter of a second late and at a different moment on each screen. One that reached its end a moment before the clock still waits there.

**Removing.** Select presentations in the library's list (Cmd/Ctrl-click adds one, Shift-click a range, Cmd/Ctrl-A all, or drag a box over them) and press **Delete** or **Backspace**. Drashti asks first, and warns when one of them is live, whose slide then stays up until it changes. **Undo** at the foot of the left column, or **Edit > Undo** (Cmd/Ctrl+Z), brings them back. Removed presentations are deleted for good after 30 days. Since Session 50 the presentation open in the middle has **Delete…** in its **⋯** menu (Presentation actions, after Edit slides; Pro Mode), with the same question and Undo; the middle then shows "Nothing chosen yet". Since Session 51 it says **Delete from the library…** while a playlist item is open there, so it is never taken for the item's own trash (which takes the item out of that playlist only), and the one question (the library's trash, its Delete key and that menu) says what happens to the playlists: "Any playlist it is in keeps its card, marked Removed from the library, and Next goes past it." ("Any playlist they are in keeps their cards, marked Removed from the library, and Next goes past them." for several). The cards are not taken out, as deleted media's are: they stay where they were, marked, until Undo. Undo of a delete from a playlist's card shows that card in the middle again while its playlist is open (`tests/e2e/placeholders.spec.ts`).

**Keeping the controls reachable.** Before an output goes on the display the operator window is on, Drashti asks, because the output would cover the controls. If an output ends up over the operator window anyway (a display unplugged or rearranged), the operator window moves to a free display when there is one. **Cmd+Shift+U** (macOS) or **Ctrl+Shift+U** (Windows), "Uncover the controls", turns off any output covering the operator window. It also works when Drashti isn't the active app, and it's in the Window menu.

**Keep these screens?** (Session 50, `src/main/outputs/keep-screens.ts`, `operator/KeepScreens.tsx`). After any change that puts an output on a display (Set Up Screens…'s **Done** or **Finish**, **Show on**, Screens' **Use this display** or **On**, a node's display, and since Session 51 Screens' **Shows**, a group's role, when the group has a screen on a display, and a key and fill pair's **Sends**), the operator window asks **Keep these screens?** for 15 seconds (`KEEP_SCREENS_MS`), the seconds left in words, with **Keep** (focused, so Enter keeps) and **Go back** (Esc too). Nothing pressed, it goes back by itself, and a notice says so. Since Session 51 going back undoes only what the change changed (`src/main/outputs/screens-setup.ts`): each change is taken just before and just after it, and Go back undoes the changes last first, each field only where it is still as the change left it, so whatever else changed in the 15 seconds stays (a group made in Screens, an output turned off or a screen renamed, another group's languages, the live Look switched); a group's languages go back in the Look the change set them in, and a group that Shows made key and fill, going back, also loses the settings becoming key and fill gave it in every Look (the words as a lower third). Before, it put back every group and screen exactly as taken before the change. Turning an output off asks nothing; a second change while one waits starts the count again, and Go back then returns to the screens before both. When the change put an output on the controls' display, that output asks the same, large in its middle, with its countdown (`output/KeepQuestion.tsx`): Enter keeps and Esc goes back (the keys reach the operator window under it), and Uncover the controls works as ever. Going back puts back the screens only (groups, screens, roles, displays, what a key and fill screen sends, on or off, and each group's languages); the sound, the theme, the stream's settings and the PINs stay as chosen. Set Up Screens… and Screens' list of displays mark the display the controls are on ("The controls are on this display"). Quitting while it waits goes back, so the next start never opens a change nobody kept, and since Session 51 so does a crash or a power cut: the changes waiting are kept in `keep-screens.json` in the data folder (written whole to a temporary file and synced to the disk, then renamed into place, and on a Mac its folder synced too; `live-state.json` is written whole and renamed the same way, but never synced) from the question until it is answered, gone back or quit, and the next start reads it before the outputs open (one that cannot be read is read once more after a moment, and left for the next start if it still cannot be), puts back what the changes changed, removes it only then (one that fails to go back stays for the next start), and says "Drashti stopped before the change to the screens was kept, so they went back to how they were before it."; one that is damaged or another version's is dropped, said in the log, and so is one left when a backup is restored and in use at that start. One that cannot be removed as it is answered (a file held open for a moment on Windows) is named answered in `keep-screens.json.answered` beside it, so the next start never goes back over a change that was kept. And since Session 51 no dialog covers the question: it is drawn a layer above the others (`Dialog`'s `onTop`), and one opened while it waits (Help › Guides…, the keys sheet) goes under it (`ui/dialog-stack.ts`), so the question keeps the keyboard and Enter and Esc still answer it; once it is answered, the keyboard goes to that dialog. Keep these screens? came from Priyansh choosing the controls' display as the audience picture in Set Up Screens… by mistake, with no way back. Tests not about it never ask (`DRASHTI_TEST_KEEP_SCREENS=0`, from the helpers): `tests/e2e/keep-screens.spec.ts`.

**Audience and Stage** (Session 50, `src/main/outputs/screen-switches.ts`, `src/shared/screen-switches.ts`, `operator/ScreenSwitches.tsx`). Two switches in Pro Mode's top bar beside **Screens**, **Audience** and **Stage**, each saying On or Off in words (Off in the warning colour too), with **View › Audience** and **View › Stage** (ticks, with Cmd+1 and Cmd+2 beside them since Session 51) and **Cmd+1** and **Cmd+2** (**Ctrl+1** and **Ctrl+2** on Windows; the window's own keys, not while typing in a field or with a dialog open). The menu reads those two keys from the keymap (`shownInMenu`) and shows them with `registerAccelerator: false`: Windows' menu then shows a key without binding it, but a Mac menu binds every key it shows, so the window takes every press of them, the ones it lets go by too (held, in a field, while composing, under a dialog), and each press acts once, by the window (`keyListener` in `operator/useKeymap.ts`; `tests/e2e/function-keys.spec.ts`). Away from the operator window, on a Mac: Window for Zoom…, whose page has no keys, stops them before its page and the menu see them, and under one of the system's own boxes (a question, a file dialog), where the menu still answers them, a choice of Audience or Stage within 0.7 s of the last goes by, so a held key changes the screens once (`src/main/shown-keys.ts`). **Audience** covers every output of this computer whose group shows the audience picture, key and fill included; **Stage** every output showing the stage view. Off closes those windows, so their displays go back to the computer; the show, LIVE, the other switch's screens, the stream, recording and nodes carry on. On opens them again on the same displays, asking nothing. Since Session 51, as a switch goes off, what each of its screens shows and where is remembered (`ScreensService.setSwitch`), and while it is off a screen just as it was then counts as showing nothing: turning it off and on again in Screens meanwhile asks nothing either, and the switch on puts it back where it was. A screen that was off as the switch went off and is turned on meanwhile, one added or given another display (the controls' display too, after Cover the controls), or one whose group's role or what it sends changes, is asked about at that change (Keep these screens?), as Session 50 had it, and so is a change that puts another picture there (the hall's screen made a stage screen). Turning one off asks first while its screens show something ("Turn Audience off?": "The hall's screens will show this computer's desktop."; Stage asks whenever a stage screen is open), with **Cancel** and **Turn off**; since Session 51 it asks too in the window's first moments, before it knows which screens are open (the safe side), and View's own box asks in the same words and case (`switchOffQuestion` and `TURN_OFF`). When an output covers the controls, that output shows the question too since Session 51, large in its middle as Keep these screens? does (`output/SwitchQuestion.tsx`: "Enter turns Audience off · Esc cancels"): the window's question under it has **Turn off** focused for the Enter that reaches it (an output never takes the keyboard), the uncover key taking that output off puts Cancel back in focus, and should the window load again or its page stop, the question goes from the output too. While one is off, the status bar says "Audience off" or "Stage off" with **Turn on** (in Simple Mode too, its only way to change them, and only on), Screens' rows say "Switched off" and how they come back, and the screens dashboard says "Audience off" in a neutral tone, not as a failure, and since Session 51 "This computer's desktop" where the screen's picture was (the last picture is forgotten, and one taken as its window closed is never sent, so on again the card waits for a new one). With Phones or the PINs on (Announcements or the role chip in the top bar) and the window under 1680 px wide, Themes, Stream, Phones, Announcements (its count still in sight), Simple Mode and the role chip show their icon alone since Session 51, their words kept for screen readers and said in their tooltips (`operator/top-bar.ts`), so the top bar fits at 1024 and 1280 wide; Screens, the two switches and the states (LIVE, ON AIR, the network) keep their words. Below 1280 px, the network's badge then says how many devices alone beside its icon (its words for screen readers, and when the mouse rests on it) and the ? beside Stream, Phones and Announcements go, so ON AIR and REC (or "On air · reconnecting", or No sound) fit beside it at 1024; LIVE's line is cut at its end, if it must be, never drawn over the buttons. Both are on at every start: off is never remembered. They are an operator's, not an admin's, so a volunteer can give the projector back to the laptop in a hurry; a phone is told which are off (`GET /api/v1/hall`'s `switches`) and cannot turn them. Uncover the controls works as before. `tests/e2e/audience-stage.spec.ts`.

**A display that drops for a moment** (Sessions 31–36, ROB-4). Projectors and HDMI converters often drop for a second or two as they are switched or warm up. When a screen's display goes away, its output window is hidden, not closed, for up to 10 seconds (`DISPLAY_DROP_MS`, `src/main/outputs/output-manager.ts`); if the display comes back in that time (matched as at every start), the same window goes back on it with its bounds set again, never loaded again: no warm-up and no black. Gone for longer, it closes as before, and a screen turned off meanwhile closes at once. `tests/e2e/display-drop.spec.ts` drops a pretend display and brings it back within 2 s (`DRASHTI_TEST_DISPLAY_CHANGES`).

**A GPU process that stops** (Sessions 31–36, ROB-5). Chromium starts another by itself, but the videos it was decoding (macOS and Windows decode H.264 there) fail with "video decode error" and stayed blank. Main now tells every window that draws the show, and each video that failed (a background, or one on a slide) loads again at a new address and plays from the engine's time (`watchGpuRestarts`, `src/renderer/src/render/media-attempts.ts`). A second stop within 10 minutes is told to the operator once (`src/main/gpu-stops.ts`). The method that proves it: Chromium's own `chrome://gpucrash`, loaded from the main process in a window that is never shown (`tests/e2e/gpu-crash.spec.ts`, with a 720p H.264 video; CI's virtual machines decode in software, so there it shows no harm, and the red was seen on a Mac with a GPU).

**Trying Drashti at the mandir.** `docs/parallel-run.md` is the volunteers' guide to the parallel run: checking the Mac's macOS version, installing Drashti from the download links above, importing the ProPresenter library, setting up screens and sound, running a sabha from a playlist, falling back to ProPresenter, saving diagnostics, the performance check and the Windows checks, and a card of the keys. Three guides go with it (Session 15): `docs/operator-guide.md` for the operators (a sabha in Pro Mode from switching on to switching off), `docs/admin-guide.md` for whoever sets Drashti up and looks after it (the two computers, updates and one version on both, roles and PINs and resetting a forgotten one, screens and Looks, backups, the node, phones, firewalls and a fixed address, Shastra texts and calendars, the idle rotation, music and markers, fonts, the checks, and falling back), and `docs/setup-day.md`, the setup day's checklist from the audit to the fallback drill.

Every shortcut is defined in one file, `src/shared/keymap.ts`. Since Session 50 they are the keys the mandir's operators already press (their program's menus, photographed on 22 Oct 2026): F1 Clear all, F2 Clear slide, F3 Clear background and F4 Clear props as before; F5 Clear audio and F6 Clear messages, and F7 Clear ticker and F8 Clear masks, changed places; **F12** puts the logo up, beside **L**; and **Cmd+1** and **Cmd+2** (**Ctrl+1** and **Ctrl+2** on Windows) turn Audience and Stage off and on. The keys sheet's last line says "These keys follow what the operators already press", and with the single-letter keys off the Logo keeps F12 (Simple Mode's key line: "Logo F12 or L", with them on). Since Session 51 the layer bar's clears go in the order of their keys (`operator/layer-clears.ts`, held to the keymap by its unit test): Clear all F1 (or Put it back in its place), Slide F2, Background F3, Props F4, Audio F5, Messages F6, Ticker F7 and Masks F8, then **?**, Logo F12 and Black-out B (Session 50 had moved the keys and left the buttons where they were). Every button shows its key at every width: a clear's under its word, its icon beside them from 1280 px wide; below 1440 px Clear all's, the Logo's and Black-out's are under their words too, a size smaller, so the bar fits a 1024-wide window (1008 on Windows, the smallest Drashti allows) in every state, the Logo and Black-out on and Put it back offered, with no sideways scrollbar; from 1440 px those three have their key beside their words, as before. A lit clear's dot sits at its bottom right, by its key. Simple Mode's big buttons' column is 28rem (at 22rem "Black-out is on" and "Logo is on" side by side ran out of the window), Back and Next still fitting a 1008-wide window. `src/main/menu-keys.test.ts` reads every menu as it is built (Main's and a node's, Pro and Simple Mode, both systems, diagnostics on and off) and every window's own key handlers, so no menu item answers a key the page answers but that key's own item, and nothing else answers the operators' keys. Since Session 51 it closes its gaps, each shown by a planted fault the old test passed: it checks every key of the keymap, not only F12, Cmd+1 and Cmd+2, each menu key pressed as the page's own `keyMatches` hears it, with the keys that window answers; tells an item's own key by the item's id (Undo, Uncover the Controls, View › Audience and Stage), never by words in its label; reads every spelling of a key (Cmd+num1, a modifier in any case, Esc, Return, Plus, "?" as Shift+/); tells a key shown but unbound (`registerAccelerator: false`) apart on each system, and then wants the keymap's `shownInMenu`; fails on a key, a submenu or a list of items it cannot read (one made from a part it does not name), rather than passing it over; catches `CommandOrControl+1`, `Ctrl+2`, `Shift+F12`, key codes and `Numpad1` in the code; keeps a table of the menu roles' keys copied by hand (`ROLE_KEYS`, from Electron 44.4.5) for the Electron major Drashti is built with, 44, so a new major fails it until they are read again (a patch keeps them); fails on a menu built outside `menu.ts` or a system-wide key written out; and checks that a key the page leaves to the menu (Undo's Mod+Z) is there, in both modes. It found one real overlap: on a Mac, Simple Mode's page took Edit › Redo's Shift+Cmd+Z as Mod+Z and put back what Clear all took down. So since Session 51 a letter with Cmd, Ctrl or Alt matches only with Shift as its key has it (`keyMatches` in `src/shared/keymap.ts`); a letter alone (B, L) still matches with Shift or without (Caps Lock, Shift held), as a digit with Cmd or Ctrl and "?" do, which some keyboards need Shift to type; Mod+F likewise no longer answers Shift+Cmd+F (Ctrl+Shift+F). `tests/e2e/function-keys.spec.ts` (Session 51) presses F5, F6, F7 and F8 for real, each taking off only its own layer on the engine, the audience screen and the audio player, and F12, which covers the picture with the logo and uncovers it again (in Simple Mode too). On a Mac laptop F12, like F1 to F8, may need Fn unless "Use F1, F2, etc. keys as standard function keys" is on; a Windows laptop may need Fn Lock.

**The guides inside Drashti** (Sessions 31–36). **Help › Guides…** shows the operator's, presenter's, admin's and setup day's guides (`docs/operator-guide.md`, `docs/presenter-guide.md`, `docs/admin-guide.md`, `docs/setup-day.md`), in English, as they came with this Drashti, and, as Drashti's own screens, never naming the old presentation program (the `.pro6` and `.pro` files are named instead; `src/main/guides.test.ts`): `electron-builder.yml` ships the four and exactly the 53 pictures they show beside the app (`resources/guides`; `src/main/guides.test.ts` keeps the list equal to what the guides show, and `scripts/check-installers.mjs` checks every installed app for them, byte for byte). The main process reads them (`src/main/guides.ts`) for the operator window only, through the bridge (`guides:list`, `guides:read`): they are never served to phones, and nothing is fetched (`tests/e2e/guides.spec.ts` stops and counts every request off the computer: none). They are drawn by Drashti's own small Markdown reader (`src/shared/markdown.ts`), element by element and never as HTML, so an address or a `<name>` is only words; a picture comes from the guides' folder only (`drashti-media://guide/<name>.png`, a name a guide shows), with its size read from its PNG header so its room is kept before it loads. A search finds the passages holding every word typed (each the start of a word, as the library's search folds them), by section; a result opens its guide there, marked. Up to three curated answers come first (`src/shared/guide-answers.ts`: 29 questions asked as "When …, what do I do?", each answered by one passage, shown in its guide's own words, and offered only where that guide can be read): the answers holding every word typed, the fewest other words first, so an answer's own words always find it first (`src/main/guide-answers.test.ts` resolves each to exactly one passage, never a heading and never another answer's, and searches each by its question and by its passage's first words). Simple Mode sees the operator's and the presenter's guides only (the admin's holds the PIN reset), and the main process refuses the other two there. It is a dialog like the keys sheet: Esc closes it, and the show's keys wait while it is open.

## Streaming and recording

**Setting up.** **Stream** in the header opens the Stream panel: the stream's picture (the **Program**) as a live preview, the sound level, the layout and the controls. **Stream settings** holds the profiles. Each has a name, the **address** (for YouTube `rtmps://a.rtmps.youtube.com/live2`, from YouTube Studio > Go live > Stream), the **stream key** (pasted once and kept in the system's secure storage: the settings only ever say "A key is saved", with Replace and Remove; without secure storage Drashti says so and keeps no key at all, never as plain text), the **internet** preset, the **camera** (or a capture card that shows up as one), the **sound input** (the mixer's line in), a **sound delay** of up to a second to line the sound up with the camera's picture, and whether **Drashti's own sound** (videos and audio cues) goes into the stream, for a mandir whose mixer cannot send the hall's sound back. The layout, camera, sound input and the stream's languages are also on **YouTube**'s card in **Screens** (Session 26: the stream is the third screen, beside the projector and the stage screen), and in the setup wizard's Stream step: one setting, shown in each place. Its group is there from the first start (an older library keeps its group and its name), has no displays and cannot be deleted. Opening Screens starts nothing: the card shows a still of what the stream would show until it is on air or recording, or the operator asks for the camera and sound input lists. On a Mac the system asks once before Drashti may use the camera and the sound input; if it was refused, or Windows' privacy settings block them, the Stream panel says where to turn them on.

| Preset            | Picture             | Video          | Sound                       | Keyframes | Needs an upload of          |
| ----------------- | ------------------- | -------------- | --------------------------- | --------- | --------------------------- |
| Good internet     | 1920 × 1080, 30 fps | 6 Mbps         | AAC 128 kbps, 48 kHz stereo | every 2 s | 8 Mbps or more              |
| Weak internet     | 1280 × 720, 30 fps  | 3 Mbps         | AAC 128 kbps, 48 kHz stereo | every 2 s | 4 to 8 Mbps                 |
| Several platforms | 1920 × 1080, 30 fps | 5 Mbps to each | AAC 128 kbps, 48 kHz stereo | every 2 s | about 7 Mbps for each place |

YouTube's own table (October 2026) asks for at least 5 Mbps at 1080p30 (14 recommended) and 3 Mbps at 720p30 (8 recommended), a keyframe every 2 seconds and constant bitrate.

**A limiter** (Session 31, MED-3). Before AAC, every stream and recording goes through FFmpeg's `alimiter` at -1 dBFS with its own level setting off (`src/main/stream/worker/encoders.ts`), so a loud desk, or Drashti's own sound mixed in with the microphone (whose sum can pass full scale), never clips on YouTube or in a recording; quieter sound passes as it is. There is no setting. In the pipeline's test a full-scale tone and one 3.5 dB louder both read back at -1.0 dBFS, and a quiet tone (RMS -17 dBFS) changed by 0.003 dB. It limits the samples' peaks: a very high tone can still peak a little above between samples once AAC has made it (an 8 kHz tone 3.5 dB over full scale read back at +0.8 dBFS on the dev Mac). The Stream panel's meter shows the mix as it comes, before the limiter.

**The layouts.** **Camera and words**: the camera full frame, the live slide's words as a lower third in the stream group's languages (chosen and ordered as on the hall's screens), and a picture or video full frame when one goes live (a media item, or a slide with a picture and no words), with the picture-in-picture box as the hall has it since Session 51, over the props (never over the camera); while a box is up, a video input behind is part of that picture too. **Slides**: what an audience screen shows, in the stream group's languages. Switch at any time, on air too. What the hall's controls do to the stream:

|                         | Camera and words                                                                       | Slides          |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------- |
| A slide with words      | Camera, with the words as a lower third                                                | As the hall     |
| A picture or video live | That picture, full frame; with a box up, as the hall has it (a video input behind too) | As the hall     |
| Clear slide, Clear all  | The camera alone (props and messages as the hall)                                      | As the hall     |
| Black-out, Logo         | For the hall only: the camera stays, the words go                                      | Black, the logo |
| Props, messages         | Shown (messages along the top)                                                         | Shown           |
| Picture in picture      | With a full-frame picture only (Session 51)                                            | As the hall     |
| Masks                   | Left out (they are for the hall's screens)                                             | As the hall     |
| The stage message       | Never                                                                                  | Never           |

**Several platforms at once** (Session 28, decision 18). A profile can send the same stream to up to two more places beside its own address, each with its own address and its own key (**Also send to another platform** in Stream settings; Facebook's address must be `rtmps://`). There is one encode; each place has its own connection, reconnecting on its own, so one place dropping never stops the others, the recording or the encoder. ON AIR stays while any place is on air, with how many of them (**On air · 1 of 2**); the panel lists each place and names the one reconnecting. Drashti tests no internet speed: the settings say the upload the places need at the preset chosen (the table), to compare with a speed test on that computer. The other places live in `stream_destinations` (migration 41), never their keys, which the key store keeps by each place's id. A test streams only to 127.0.0.1: any other address is refused, loudly (the guard every end-to-end run sets).

**Going live.** **Go live…** asks first (a stream is public), then the header says **ON AIR** (not LIVE, which means the hall's screens). The panel shows the time on air, the connection (Good, Struggling, Reconnecting), what is being sent, frames a second, frames dropped, reconnections and the encoder in use. **End the stream…** asks too. No key starts or ends a stream. If the connection drops or FFmpeg stops, Drashti connects again by itself after 1, 2, 4, 8, 15 and then every 30 seconds, and says so; a connection that lasted 30 s starts the waits again.

**The stream server's certificate** (Session 23). On every secure connection (`rtmps://`, as YouTube's address is), FFmpeg checks the stream server's certificate against the certificate authorities it trusts, and Drashti never turns that check off. How it knows which authorities to trust:

- **macOS:** Drashti gives FFmpeg the system's own list, `/etc/ssl/cert.pem`, which macOS keeps up to date (`src/main/stream/tls.ts`). The bundled FFmpeg (OpenSSL) otherwise looks only in its builder's folder, so before Session 23 a stream from any Mac other than the builder's was refused ("certificate verify failed") and never went on air. The same applies on Apple silicon and Intel Macs. If the file is missing, the log says so.
- **Windows:** the bundled FFmpeg (GnuTLS) uses Windows' own certificate store, as other programs do, and is given nothing. CI's Windows showed it refusing a made-up authority, then going on air once that authority was in the machine's Trusted Root store.

If the check fails anyway (a wrong date or time on the computer, or a network that intercepts secure connections), the Stream panel says "The stream server's certificate could not be checked, so nothing was sent", not only Reconnecting. `src/main/stream/worker/rtmps.test.ts` checks this with each bundled FFmpeg the computer can run (on CI's Mac, the Intel build too, under Rosetta). It runs against a stand-in on the same computer whose certificate a made-up authority signed (`src/main/stream/testing/made-up-ca.ts`): refused without that authority, on air once FFmpeg is given its file. Nothing goes to the internet.

**Recording.** Choose a folder once, then **Record**: with the stream or without it. Recordings are Matroska files (`Drashti <date> <time>.mkv`, which VLC and IINA open and QuickTime Player does not) that play even if Drashti stops mid-way, and a dropped connection never stops one. Drashti keeps 2 GB free on that disk: the panel shows the free space and about how long the recording can go on, and when space runs low it stops the recording with a message, and the stream goes on. **REC** shows in the header.

**Long recordings in parts** (Session 31, MED-2). A part holds at most 3.5 GB (under FAT32's 4 GB for a file): then the recording goes on in `<name> part 2.mkv`, `part 3`… from the next picture, each part with its own header and starting at 0, so each plays on its own and none is lost or doubled between them (`src/main/stream/worker/pipeline.ts`; the soak's 3 hours at the weak preset make one). A part is opened only under a name no file has (the first free number), and the encoder starting again also carries on in the next part, as before. About every 10 seconds the worker asks the disk to keep what it has written (`fdatasync`, one at a time, never waited for), so a power cut loses seconds, not minutes. As a recording starts, Drashti looks at its drive: on FAT32 (a USB stick as it comes; macOS's mount list, Linux's mounts, Windows' DriveInfo) or with room for less than an hour before the 2 GB it keeps, it says so plainly in the status bar and the Stream panel; when it cannot tell, it says nothing. Parts have no index for seeking (Cues), as before: players find their way through them.

**After a crash.** If Drashti stops unexpectedly while on air or recording and starts again within 5 minutes, it goes live (and records) again by itself, with the same profile, and says so. Later than that it only offers to, in the Stream panel. A recording always starts a new file; the earlier one is kept as it is, and plays. A quit on purpose ends the stream and does not resume it: since Session 23 the stream service saves that it ended before the library closes, and a start after a clean quit never resumes the stream, whatever `stream-state.json` says. Before Session 23, quitting while on air left Drashti running with no window and still on air (the stream's page opened again as the operator window closed, which stopped the quit); a second quit then left the stream marked live, and a start within 5 minutes went live again by itself.

**No sound** (Session 31). While on air or recording, the stream worker measures the sound it gives the encoder, a second at a time (the RMS of both channels, `src/main/stream/worker/sound-meter.ts`): what the page sends and the silence that keeps time when it sends nothing alike, so a stream page that stalled counts too. After 20 seconds below -50 dBFS, **No sound** shows beside ON AIR and REC in Pro and Simple Mode, with one notice under the header (never over the show controls) saying what to check; a microphone that is unplugged (or that the system blocks, or that would not start) shows it once it has been gone for 3 seconds with nothing heard since (`src/main/stream/no-sound.ts`). It goes as soon as a second is heard again, and the log says so once when it starts and once when it ends. Drashti never switches the microphone by itself, and says nothing while neither on air nor recording. Chromium's fake microphone (a short beep every half second) never raises it.

**Simple Mode** shows ON AIR and REC (and No sound), and the main process refuses starting, ending or changing the stream.

**The window for Zoom** (Session 48). **Window for Zoom…** (in the Stream panel, and View › Window for Zoom…, Pro Mode) opens a normal window titled "Drashti for Zoom" that shows the stream's picture exactly, at 16:9, and nothing of Drashti's own, for Zoom's **Share Screen**; Zoom's microphone is the mixer's input (Drashti sends Zoom no sound).
